# Golden-set labeling worksheet (plan §12)

250 questions sampled from the Stage 4 canonical current Q&A pairs, stratified by year × product family (2025–2026 weighted for currency), with coverage guarantees for the FAQ families and evergreen topics. For each item, fill in the three labeled fields.

**Rubric** (per §12):

- **Points to hit** — 2–5 bullets a correct, complete answer must cover. Derive them from the source answer below; write them as verifiable statements, not topics.
- **Expected sources** — which authorities a correct answer should cite: authority 1 = products.json (approved claims, quote verbatim), 2 = PDSRG, 3 = QA canonical, 4 = podcast. Candidates are prefilled; check the ones that apply, add others.
- **Forbidden** — claim language the answer must NOT use (disease treatment/cure/prevention beyond approved copy, medication or condition guidance — anything on the §8 hard-escalation list must hand off to a human instead of being answered).

The **source answer** is the scrubbed expert answer the question was sampled from — the starting point for the points-to-hit, not the required answer text. Answers may be outdated on details; flag that in the points rather than copying it.

Splits alternate dev/test (125/125); labelers label both — the split only gates when the eval harness may look at them.

**Q:** Does dotFIT have any research or commentary on the 2008 New England Journal of Medicine study comparing a Mediterranean-style diet as an alternative to a low-fat diet?

**Forbidden:**

- disease-treatment/cure/prevention language beyond products.json wording (claims are quoted, never paraphrased); medication or managed-condition guidance (escalate per §8); dosage arithmetic the source answer does not do.
- (item-specific) …

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> The bottom line: Calories count most in weight loss, but picking the foods that make up those calories that make you feel best is the key — pick the right ones for you personally and you will burn more calories (see attached Calorie is a Calorie article).
>
> The studies attached give the latest take on diet makeup and weight loss. Basically it all comes out the same long term, but the goal is to do something you can sustain because long term is generally weight regain and maybe more so for very high-protein dieters (see Macro Comp study attached).
>
> Faster weight loss with higher protein, and protein is more satiating — at least in the short term.
>
> The Mediterranean diet is probably one of the healthiest since it contains good fats and carbs with lean proteins, and that's why we have one for everyone in our program.
>
> Below are some related Q&A from our Fitness Vault (click on the links or visit the entire section of Fit-pro Q&As):
>
> Is the high protein/low carbohydrate best? Why?
> What can we do to keep people motivated and break the weight loss and gain cycle permanently?
> 35. Why are so many people still so Carb cautious?
>
> Only because they don't know the difference between good and bad carbs, and carbs overall are not as important, from an energy supply standpoint, for the average adult as they are for athletes or active exercisers. From a health standpoint carbs such as fruits, vegetables and whole grains are very important and should make up 40-60% of the diet depending on your activity levels. Unfortunately refined grains/foods and added sugars are also carbohydrates, generally less healthy and considered contributors to weight gain when it's actually the calories causing the weight gain — they just happen to be supplied in lousy food choices. So the message to stay away from carbs is to stay away from the bad carbs not the good, but the average person doesn't hear that — it's just easier to be carb conscious all the way around.
>
> And finally, most people still don't grasp the fact that total daily calories in and out are what count NOT carbs!

> Hi [CUSTOMER] (also see my comment to your #2 question).
>
> The dotFIT program works perfectly for weight/muscle gain as we use for the NFL & NBA combines and other sports venues/programs where muscle gain and performance are the combined goal.
>
> Weight/muscle gain
>
> Use the dotFIT program to get your starting calories and plug in your weight gain goal. Each week (or 2-weeks if using body fat measurements) enter your statistics as prompted and make the adjustments as described. I have also included the basic weight gain instructions here and attached the muscle stack supplement programs for you to pick from.
>
> Fat Loss
>
> For your “cutting” program I have attached the bodybuilding guidelines including supplement program that all our sponsored physique athletes use. Again, use the program to get started and make the adjustments as described in the attached document. The supplement program is within the attached document.
>
> Weight/Muscle gain
>
> In order to simultaneously increase weight/muscle and performance: males may gain up to one-half pound per week and females up to one-quarter pound per week. Beginning exercisers, children and growing teens may gain more. Additionally, if performance is not the focus, meaning size increase is the priority, you can add more calories than shown below to attempt to gain the desired weight slightly quicker. Make sure your daily diet contains 1gm of protein for each pound of lean body mass (LBM) divided 4-6 times a day including before and after training as shown in your supplement recommendation.
>
> Total daily calorie intake should be moderately above current expenditure (dotFIT program will automatically create the right menus and choose the “Athletic Menu”). If weight gain does not occur as described, you may add roughly 100 to 250 calories to your daily total (based on body size) consisting of equal amounts of carbohydrates and protein and moderate fat. For example, 20 g protein, 20 g carbohydrate, 10 g fat equaling 250 calories. If preferred, use your shakes or snacks to supply extra calories. Following the addition of extra calories, if after one week weight gain does not occur, repeat the above process*. All this is automatically taken care of using your dotFIT program.
>
> *If body fat or overall weight increases undesirably, slightly reduce daily caloric intake or add a preferred form of aerobic exercise until you achieve your desired weight trend.
>
> Hopefully you were on one of the webinars I did last week on the new powdered ExtremeCreatineXXXL. If not you can download it and see the new muscle gain stacks using this product that replaces the pills and with an upgraded formula. Link to webinars: https://www.dotfit.com/seminars_presentations
>
> All our many webcasts are located in your Trainer Console.
>
> Here is a link to the updated science all captured in your Practitioner Dietary Supplement Guide (PDSRG): ExtremeCreatineXXXL
>
> I also attached the stacks here so you can pick as you like.

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. Physiology including hormones, daily activities and appetite make subtle changes post-menopause, often making life and weight control more difficult. But the equation for losing weight is the same: eat fewer calories than you burn. To increase or at a minimum maintain lean body mass (LBM) simultaneously requires proper dietary support — i.e., supplementation.
>
> Therefore, finding a diet she can stick with is the key as well as maintaining the exercise program to complement the diet, and supplement properly so that you can feed muscle while starving body fat (see baseline supplement program below that I added AminoXXXL to help support lean body mass, especially in older adults). This protocol is what we call “favorably altering body composition,” and the goal you describe requires keeping/adding lean body mass (LBM) while losing body fat. The good news is that the dotFIT program will take you all the way. Once you create your trainer account, you can add your client and create their individualized program. I have cc'd [NAME] here to make sure you get set up properly with a trainer account. Then read below and feel free to forward this information to your client so they understand the process.
>
> Welcome to our family. Good luck. Stay tuned for [NAME] and we’ll be with you all the way.
>
> Building and/or Maintaining LBM While Losing Body fat and The dotFIT Program
>
> Building/maintaining LBM while losing body fat, depending on age and training experience (younger and less experience the easier and faster the gains), is a formula that requires a few components that you need to adhere to:
> 1) Daily menu plan that has the correct calories, foods including protein quantity matched to the body composition goal.
> 2) Dietary supplement program to feed muscle while starving fat – i.e., deliver high nutrition with little to no calories – meaning you will need at least a multivitamin and mineral (MVM) and extra protein to help recover and maintain structural and functional integrity (when calories are limited to reduce body fat, you also limit the nutrients that would otherwise be contained in those calories).
> 3) Regular unaccustomed resistance exercise.
>
> Note: Make sure you consume daily between all food and protein shake sources close to 1 gram of protein for every pound of lean body mass (LBM) or body weight (if not overweight) divided 4-5 times daily including using your protein shake before and after the workouts. See below.
>
> Fitness, Health, Activity Recovery, Body Fat Loss & LBM Support Program - Level-1 with AminoXXXL
>
> Daily:
> - dotFIT Multivitamin & Mineral for your age and gender https://www.dotfit.com/Womens-Multivitamin (or https://www.dotfit.com/Over50MV if over 50years)
>   Take as directed
>
> Daily as needed:
> - WheySmooth https://www.dotfit.com/WheySmooth-Chocolate, Vanilla or Unflavored* or other favorite dotFIT protein mix https://www.dotfit.com/store/nutrition
>   Use as directed to make sure you get ~1gm of protein per pound of LBM (or weight if not overweight) daily from all sources including foods divided 3-5 times daily
>   If exercising use ~30-35gm 30-40min before workout & repeat dose 30min after post workout AB dose (see below)
>   Can be used as a supplement to a meal or by itself (or mixed with desired supporting contents) to shore up total daily protein needs
>
> - Super Calcium https://www.dotfit.com/super-calcium (read all food labels for calcium content including dotFIT protein shakes)
>   Use if you do not meet the daily needs of calcium (~1000-1200 mgs) from food/shake intake
>   Females take 1 or 2 daily with meals; if you need to take 2, take one with AM meal and the 2nd with PM meal; Males take 1 only if necessary
>
> - Super Omega 3 https://www.dotfit.com/superomega3
>   Take 1 daily with meal if not consuming 2-4 svgs/wk of fatty fish
>
> Workout days
> - AminoXXXL (AX) https://www.dotfit.com/aminoxxxl-lemonade , Blue Raspberry, watermelon and Vegan Tropical Colada
>   Take 1 scoop ~5-10min before and may continue to drink during activity/workout
>   Take 1 scoop immediately following workout/activity
>
> - WheySmooth* use as directed to get:
>   30-35gm/protein 30-40min before workout & repeat same dose ~30min after post exercise AX dose
>
> *May substitute favorite dotFIT Nutrition High Protein Bar based on venue convenience

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us, and we've got you covered! No matter how much you work out or what exercises you perform, you won’t get rid of belly fat without a proper diet. You need to support your muscle (LBM) while losing body fat. Following the dotFIT program, including the supplement program below, will get rid of the belly fat, support LBM and give you the energy you need while pursuing the goal because you will be feeding your muscle while starving body fat. Please read all below and choose one of the 3 options to get started - and reach out any time to one of our phone coaches (cc'd [NAME] here) for more information or help.
>
> Thanks again and good luck on your fitness journey. We will be with you all the way!
>
> Spot reducing note
> Losing body fat anywhere on the body requires consuming less calories than you burn regardless of the type of diet you consume. The goal is to find a diet that you’re comfortable with so you can stick with it until you reach your goal knowing there is no such thing as “spot reducing” – i.e. fat leaves the body from wherever it’s genetically programmed to lose it, and sometimes the places you want it to leave from first does not happen till later – but it will happen if you stay in the calorie deficit. You do this by eating less, moving more or a combination of the two until you reach the goal.
>
> Building or Maintain Muscle While Losing Body fat and The dotFIT Program
> Building/maintaining muscle while losing body fat, depending on age and training experience (younger and less experience the easier and faster the gains), is not often easy to accomplish because the formula requires a few components that you need to adhere to: 1) daily menu plan that has the correct calories, foods including protein quantity matched to the body composition goal; 2) dietary supplement program to feed muscle while starving fat – i.e. deliver high nutrition with little to no calories - meaning you will need at least a multivitamin and mineral (MVM) and extra protein to help recover and maintain structural and functional integrity (when calories are limited to reduce body fat, you also limit the nutrients that would otherwise be contained in those calories); 3) regular unaccustomed resistance exercise. Additionally, we placed the minimal supplement program we use for exercisers to healthily speed recovery and results while supporting health and fat loss.
>
> Note: Make sure you consume daily between all food and protein shake sources close to 1 gram of protein for every pound of lean body mass (LBM) or body weight (if not overweight) divided 4-5 times daily including using your protein shake before and after the workouts. See below. And as you progress, you can add additional supplementation if you continue to want to lose more body fat and/or gain more muscle by regularly updating your program with your weight/body fat measurements (it will automatically update the diet and supplements) - or use our supplement screener tool: https://www.dotfit.com/supplements-screener
>
> 3 options:
> You can visit one of our licensed health clubs using the club finder http://www.dotfit.com/ClubFinder.aspx The expert fitness professionals in our licensed health clubs can give you direct service for all your fitness goals including nutrition needs (non-clinical).
>
> Alternately, if a club visit is not a viable option, you can receive all your diet, supplement and muscle gain (and/or fat loss) needs online from the dotFIT program. Go to the dotFIT home page and log in (using the icon in far upper right corner) and you will come to this page: http://www.dotfit.com/new-user-register (You may just click this link for now). From there simply follow instructions creating your program and setting your goal. The dotFIT program will give you the meal plan outline you can alter as desired with the proper calories, carbohydrates, fats and protein to get started, track intake and monitor results. It will also give you your supplement recommendations (I placed one below also) and exactly how to use them. Every week when you enter your weight and/or bodyfat, the program will tell you what to do until you reach your goal including recreating menus as necessary. Once you create the program and get menus, you can go to our recipe section on the dotFIT website to get recipes with meal preparation instructions including meal replacement recipes: https://www.dotfit.com/learn/recipes. There is also a complete exercise program you can use to create the proper program to accomplish your goal – and again, just answer the simple questions including entering your goal, and your exercise program will be created. However, if possible, we recommend one of our trainers to help you get started. On the dotFIT website you’ll also see a category called “Learn” where you will find many frequently asked questions and answers: https://www.dotfit.com/learn
>
> Reply again to this email or call us at [PHONE] and we will put you together with one of our phone coaches.
>
> General Health, Recovery, Weight Control/bodyfat Reduction/Toning and Performance Supplement Program
> Click on product links for a brief product description including short video
>
> Daily:
> dotFIT Multivitamin & Mineral for your age and gender. If 50yrs or older use Over50 unless noted below; if female under 50 use Women’s; Males and very active females (competitive training protocols) under 50 use 2-Active, with both genders continuing use till 65yrs if still training competitively at which time every person will use Over50; children under 12yr use Kids; children 12-17yr use 1-Active.
> Take as directed with meals
> WheySmooth or other favorite dotFIT protein mix (based on caloric allotment for goal)
> Use as directed to make sure you get ~1 gm of protein per pound of LBM (or weight if not overweight) daily from all sources including foods divided 3-5 times daily
> Can be used as a supplement to a meal or by itself (or mixed with desired supporting contents) to shore up total daily protein needs
>
> As needed:
> Super Calcium (read all food labels for calcium content including dotFIT protein shakes)*
> Use if you do not meet the daily needs of calcium (~1000-1200 mgs) from food/shake intake
> Females take 1 or 2 daily with meals; if you need to take 2, take one with AM meal and the 2nd with PM meal; Males take 1 only if necessary
> Super Omega 3
> Take 1-2 daily with meal if not consuming 8-16oz/wk fatty fish (take 1 if >8oz/wk but <16; 2 if ≤8oz); not necessary if consuming ≥16oz/wk unless for athletic recovery purposes, then increase as directed
> *All food labels including Protein Powders must contain calcium levels by serving so always check
>
> Workout Days:
> WheySmooth or other favorite dotFIT protein mix
> Take 1 scoop ~30-40 minutes before workout
> Take 1 scoop immediately following workout

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. We do not sell individual iron supplements because supplementing iron beyond the normal small amount in a complete Multivitamin and Mineral Formula (MVM) should always be determined by blood work. When you received your blood work, your doctor should have recommended an iron supplement. It sounds like the one you purchased was a form of iron that often causes constipation or stomach distress. There are many different forms of iron supplements. Iron bisglycinate made by Solgar, in 25mg capsules, may be gentler for your stomach. But if not, consult your doctor again, and be mindful that only your future bloodwork can determine the proper dosage or cessation.
>
> Hope this helps and have a great weekend!

> Note: I think everyone saw the report about erythritol and potential for blood clotting in already diseased or at-risk people using high amounts. Therefore, most likely you would have to eat many foods daily with erythritol and already have cardiovascular disorders to potentially increase risks of clotting. See below from the study:
> https://www.cnn.com/2023/02/27/health/zero-calorie-sweetener-heart-attack-stroke-wellness/index.html
>
> “There appears to be a clotting risk from using erythritol,” Freeman said. “Obviously, more research is needed, but in an abundance of caution, it might make sense to limit erythritol in your diet for now.”
>
> In response to the study, the Calorie Control Council, told CNN that “the results of this study are contrary to decades of scientific research showing reduced-calorie sweeteners like erythritol are safe, as evidenced by global regulatory permissions for their use in foods and beverages,” said [NAME], the council’s executive director. Therefore, the results “should not be extrapolated to the general population, as the participants in the intervention were already at increased risk for cardiovascular events.”

*thread_date: 2023-05-19 · topics: Micronutrient insufficiency, RDA, SRNR, Tolerable Upper Limit · source: `a689a2b5145d27eb` 2023/Vitamin RDAs, over RDAs, diagram, sweet spot, benefits diagram from article.docx*

**Expected sources:**

- [ ] QA `a689a2b5145d27eb` — 2023/Vitamin RDAs, over RDAs, diagram, sweet spot, benefits diagram from article.docx (authority 3)
- [ ] podcast segment (authority 4)
- [ ] other: …

**Forbidden:**

- disease-treatment/cure/prevention language beyond products.json wording (claims are quoted, never paraphrased); medication or managed-condition guidance (escalate per §8); dosage arithmetic the source answer does not do.
- (item-specific) …

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> I’m assuming you are referring to the RDAs? As you know, many RDAs for vitamins and minerals are considered too low such as D, C, E, most B vitamins, etc., and most have a wide range of safety, and possibly more benefits up the Tolerable Upper Limit (UL). Therefore, somewhere between the RDAs and the UL is the sweet spot like you have seen in all our lectures (see below). Call me if you need more information.
>
> Have a great weekend!
>
> Appendix 2 Summary Figure of Proposed VM Intakes
> Range for Potential Maximum Benefits (The Safe and Recommended Nutrient Range [SRNR])
>
> Readers are reminded that because of the synergy of essential nutrition throughout all phases of life, vitamins and essential minerals are dependent on each other in all areas of metabolism and therefore insufficiencies of a single vitamin or mineral can cause a negative domino effect, albeit over time – i.e., insidious health risk effects.
>
> The SRNR is depicted below using the data from Table 2 and shows potential overall cellular functioning benefits at different VM concentrations ranging from deficiency to recommended. Typical known U.S. intakes from food alone are shown in the yellow area, and depending on the vitamin or mineral, reach about 40-85% benefits if the RDA is 100%. The “sweet-spot” is shown in green, which, based on the DRIs would be the ~RDA to slightly below the NOAEL and definitely below the UL. Maintaining this sweet spot should be a lifelong goal and entirely possible using properly designed formula incorporating the criteria described in this article to fill in the known gaps, depicted as an average by the yellow/orange area.
>
> Definitions
>
> Nutrient (vitamins and essential minerals [VM]): a substance that provides nourishment essential for growth and the maintenance of life
>
> VM deficiency: gross under-consumed nutrient leading to overt symptoms that left untreated (repletion) will cause eventual sickness or death
>
> VM insufficiency: nutrient intakes below the RDA or Estimated Average Requirement (EAR) that left untreated (repletion), may not have overt symptoms, but insidious outcomes related to short and long-term development and health such as disease manifesting so gradually, it would be established before becoming evident, thus likely irreversible.
>
> RDA: Recommended Dietary Allowance - the dietary intake level that is sufficient (not necessarily optimal) to meet the nutrient requirement of nearly all (97 to 98 percent) healthy individuals in a particular life stage and gender group.
>
> AI: Adequate Intake - a recommended intake value based on observed or experimentally determined approximations or estimates of nutrient intake by a group (or groups) of healthy people that are assumed to be adequate—used when an RDA cannot be determined.
>
> UL: Tolerable Upper Intake Level - the highest level of nutrient intake that is likely to pose no risk of adverse health effects for almost all individuals in the general population.
>
> NOAEL: No Observed Adverse Effect Level - the highest concentration or amount/dose of a substance found NOT to cause an adverse reaction.
>
> LOAEL: The Lowest Observed Adverse Effect Level - the lowest concentration or amount/dose of a substance found to cause an adverse reaction.

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
> Yes, we do, but only if it is a blossoming athlete who is going to get to the next level, is consuming the proper diet, and we have the parents' permission. The products you name are perfectly safe and healthful for a 17yr old normal healthy human, but from a business perspective we don’t promote them to minors. In other words, there is no scientific reason for not using these products in youth athletes, but there is the “business CYA” side. Therefore, it is up to the parents to approve when they have all the data, which you can supply through your Practitioner Dietary Supplement Reference Guide (PDSRG) in your trainer console. I placed an FAQ below with more information if necessary. Further, the products you name have all been used safely for years with minors for various clinical reasons.
> Hope this helps. Have a great weekend!
> FAQ
> Q: Can youth athlete use creatine.
> A: Proper creatine supplementation is probably safe for most healthy adolescents, but our position is to not make a recommendation until the youth elite athlete is through puberty* and then with a parent’s consent once they have consulted with a qualified professional. Below has more information on position statements by other experts. I have also placed a baseline dietary support program below that we give all youth athletes and growing and developing children ages 5-17yrs.
> ** Puberty usually occurs in girls between the ages of 10 and 14; boys 12 and 16.
> Creatine supplementation in youth and adolescence
> General rule is ≥18yrs unless a younger elite athlete is properly supervised with parent consent
> The 18yrs or older label cautions on creatine products have to do with manufacturers shielding themselves from liabilities and not based on safety reasons since science has documented CS safety in all age groups as shown discussed above. 48,49,50,[i] The latest position by the International Society of Sports Nutrition (ISSN) is the following: “If proper precautions and supervision are provided, creatine monohydrate supplementation in children and adolescent athletes is acceptable and may provide a nutritional alternative with a favorable safety profile to potentially dangerous anabolic androgenic drugs. However, we recommend that creatine supplementation only be considered for use by younger athletes who: a.) are involved in serious/competitive supervised training; b.) are consuming a well-balanced and performance enhancing diet; c.) are knowledgeable about appropriate use of creatine; and d.) do not exceed recommended dosages”.15,28 i
> If you are further interested in youth and creatine supplementation you are referred to the [NAME] et al. review “Safety of CS in Active Adolescents and Youth”Ref3

I have also cc’d [NAME] here to help clarify your needs and explain the program below and alter as necessary.
> Thanks again and reach out to [NAME] as needed.
>
> BMI is used to broadly define different weight groups in adults 20 years old or older.
> Underweight: BMI is less than 18.5
> Normal weight: BMI is 18.5 to 24.9
> Overweight: BMI is 25 to 29.9
> Obese: BMI is 30 or more
>
> Performance/Size Plan & Instructions with Creatine Monohydrate in Baseline
> Click on product links for a brief product description including short video
>
> ActiveMV - Multivitamin & Mineral Formula
> Take two (2) daily: one immediately following 1st large meal; one after final meal of the day.
>
> Super Omega 3
> Take 2 daily with meal
>
> Super Calcium (read all food labels for calcium content)
> Use if you do not meet the daily needs of calcium (~1000-1200 mgs) from food/shake intake (using FirstString or other dotFIT protein powder regularly with a typical diet, calcium intake would probably meet calcium needs without supplementing)
> If needed take 1 with a meal
>
> FirstString
> Take two (2) scoops 30-45 minutes before workout or use favorite dotFIT           bar for convenience.
> Take two (2) scoops 20-30 minutes after post workout AminoFormula dose.
> Use anytime throughout the day as a supplement to a meal or by itself mixed with desired ingredients to add protein/calories as needed to daily requirements.
> Be sure to ingest approximately 1 gram (g) of protein per pound of lean body mass (or weight if not overweight) daily from all sources including foods divided 4-5 times daily along with the pre/post shakes (the pre/post shakes combined generally supply 50-60 g of the daily protein requirement).
>
> CreatineMonohydrate Loading & Maintenance Strategy
> Mix 1 scoop (5gms) with 4-8oz of favorite fluid/shake and take 4 times daily with a protein and/or carbohydrate containing meal/drink for first 5 days. Thereafter take 1-scoop once daily to maintain stores. To help maximize creatine uptake, split doses throughout the day with meals/drinks containing protein and/or carbohydrate.
>
> Training days: take 1-scoop before or after your workout with meals/drinks. May mix with your pre & post training formula.
> Non-training days* take 1-scoop with a meal or shake
> *May substitute favorite dotFIT Nutrition High Protein Bar based on venue convenience
>
> Meal Timings
> As possible eat every 4-hours
> Large pre-training/event meal 2-3Hr before training
> Large post meal ~30-60min after last post workout supplement
> Early morning training
> Eat a large pre-training type meal the night before
> Consume only the pre-workout snack/shake before training (as shown above) & follow workout day supplement schedule above
> Tournament play (multiple games)
> <1.5Hr break: bars and hydration/electrolyte recovery drink
> 1.5-2.5Hr: small pre-training-type meal
> >2.5Hr: normal pre-training meal
>
> Fluid Recommendations*
> 16 oz 2Hr before activity (extra 8-16 oz 1hr before on hot days)
> 4-8 oz every 20 minutes during activity

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. You can gain muscle weight using any protein powder if your total daily calories are conducive to muscle gain and your resistance training program is progressive (i.e., regularly unaccustomed). However, the most commonly used protein powder for muscle gain and performance is the FirstString since it is preset with the proper carbohydrate (CHO) to protein ratio for maximizing muscle hypertrophy. That said, if you are looking for an all-natural source, such as the ones you named (All Natural WheySmooth, BestPlantProtein), all you have to do is add whatever CHO you would like to make the same ratio as in FirstString (~2CHO:1P). See below for the recommended baseline dietary support program with mealtimes for building muscle that includes the proper use of your desired protein powder (just substitute the powder you prefer for FirstString as noted). After using this baseline supplement program, and you want to take your fitness goals further, you can use our supplement screening tool to get the proper recommendations. Reach out to one of our coaches at any time for more information or questions. [NAME] is cc'd here.
>
> Thanks again and have a great day!
>
> Baseline Health, Recovery, Muscle Gain and Performance Supplement Program
> Click on product links for a brief product description including short video
>
> Daily:
>
> dotFIT Multivitamin & Mineral for your age and gender If 50yrs or older use Over50 unless noted below; if female under 50 use Women’s; Males and very active females (competitive training protocols) under 50 use 2-Active, with both genders continuing use till 65yrs if still training competitively at which time every person will use Over50; children under 12yr use Kids; children 12-17yr use 1-Active). Vegans use 1-Vegan
>
> Take as directed with meals
>
> Daily as needed:
>
> FirstString (or favorite dotFIT Protein such as All Natural WheySmooth, or BestPlantProtein)
>
> Use as directed to make sure you get ~1gm of protein per pound of LBM (or weight if not overweight) daily from all sources including foods divided 5 times daily
>
> Can be used as a supplement to a meal or by itself (or mixed with desired supporting contents) to shore up total daily protein needs
>
> SuperCalcium (read all food and shake labels) (using FirstString or other dotFIT protein powder regularly with other typical diet calcium intake would probably meet calcium needs without supplementing)
>
> Use if you do not meet the daily needs of calcium (~1000-1200 mgs) from food/shake intake
>
> Females take 1 or 2 daily with meals; if you need to take 2, take one with AM meal and the 2nd with PM meal; Males take 1 only if necessary
>
> SuperOmega-3
>
> Take 1-2 daily with meal if not consuming 8-16oz/wk fatty fish (take 1 if >8oz/wk but <16; 2 if ≤8oz); not necessary if consuming ≥16oz/wk unless for athletic recovery purposes
>
> Workout days
>
> FirstString* (or favorite dotFIT Protein such as All Natural WheySmooth, or BestPlantProtein) - use as directed to get:
>
> 25-35gm/protein 30-40min before workout & repeat same dose immediately post exercise
>
> *May substitute favorite dotFIT Nutrition High Protein Bar based on venue convenience
>
> Meal Timings
>
> As possible eat every 4-hours
>
> Large pre-training/event meal 2-3Hr before training
>
> Large post meal ~30-60min after last post workout supplement
>
> Early morning training
>
> Eat a large pre-training type meal the night before
>
> Consume only the pre-workout snack/shake before training (as shown above) & follow workout day supplement schedule above
>
> Tournament play (multiple games)
>
> <1.5Hr break: bars and hydration/electrolyte recovery drink
>
> 1.5-2.5Hr: small pre-training-type meal
>
> >2.5Hr: normal pre-training meal
>
> Fluid Recommendations*
>
> 16 oz 2Hr before activity (extra 8-16 oz 1hr before on hot days)
>
> 4-8 oz every 20 minutes during activity
>
> 20 oz for every pound of weight loss post-activity
>
> *Use electrolyte formula (e.g. Gatorade) and water as directed

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. None of the dotFIT products other than a few of the nutrition bars contain gluten. But if they are made in a facility that makes other products that contain gluten, then it must be put on the label for the people that may be highly allergic (such as in the case you mention, people with celiac disease), in case there would be a slim chance of some minute cross contamination. Again, none of the dotFIT proteins or powders contain gluten. All our dietary supplements are also gluten free and many made in facilities that are safe from cross contamination (simply read the labels). Most of the nutrition food bars are not gluten free. The peanut butter and jelly bar and the dotFIT Vegan bar are gluten free. Click here for the gluten free list in the store. And see below for more information on labeling. The labels on our products will always contain the proper information.
>

>
> FAQ
> Q: I'm a trainer and was wondering about the supplements that would be safe for our clients with peanut/wheat allergies or sensitivities (or worried about cross-contamination)? I'm trying to keep everyone safe while getting them the best supplements they can use.
> A: Please read every product label carefully as all ours contain the proper warnings (see below) including gluten, shellfish, peanut, etc., for products that are made in facilities that may also make products with peanut/wheat extracts such as one of our many powder manufacturers. Most of our pills/capsules such as fish oil, MVMs, calcium, joint & Skin support, etc., are made in facilities free of potential peanut or gluten cross-contamination and therefore, safe for your recommendations/use, thus warnings are not necessary. Last thing we want is for your clients to have a reaction, so please read all labels of products they are interested in for their fitness goals. Both our BestPlantProtein and All Natural WheySmooth your client can use as their protein supplement for their fitness goals since it’s made in the proper facilities. Therefore, I put a simple baseline supplement program below you can use with your client. You can then add as needed, making sure you read all labels as this will be published on all labels only as needed:
>
> “Allergen Warning: This product was produced in a facility that may also process ingredients containing milk, eggs, fish, shellfish, tree nuts, peanuts, wheat, and soybeans.
> Contains: Milk, Egg, and Soy (from soy lecithin)”.
>
> Note on AminoFormula:
> The Lemonade and Blue Raspberry AminoFormula are now produced in a facility that is free of shellfish, fish, shellfish, tree nuts, peanuts and wheat/gluten. This is a recent facility change so some old labels with the original warning may still be on the product. Label printings going forward will contain only an allergen warning of:  “Contains: Milk, Soy (from soy lecithin) and Egg.”
>
> General Sport Health, Activity Recovery & Performance Support Program -Level-1 (Baseline)
> Click on product links for a brief product description including short video
> Daily:
> dotFIT Multivitamin & Mineral for your age and gender (if 50yrs or older use Over50; if female under 50, use Women’s; Males and very active females under 50 use 2-Active; children under 12yr use Kids; children 12-17yr use 1-Active). Click on videos in the store under “Overview” for brief descriptions)
> Take as directed
>
> Daily as needed:
> All Natural WheySmooth, (or you could also use the BestPlantProtein powder)*
> Use as directed to make sure you get ~1gm of protein per pound of LBM (or weight if not overweight) daily from all sources including foods divided 5 times daily
> If exercising use ~25gm 30-40min before workout & repeat dose immediately post workout (see below)
> Can be used as a supplement to a meal or by itself (or mixed with desired supporting contents) to shore up total daily protein needs
> Super Calcium (read all food labels for calcium content including dotFIT protein shakes)
> Use if you do not meet the daily needs of calcium (~1000-1200 mgs) from food/shake intake

> Take 1-2 daily with meal if not consuming 8-16oz/wk fatty fish (take 1 if >8oz/wk but <16; 2 if ≤8oz)
>
> Workout days

> Take 1- scoop 30-40min before workout & repeat same dose immediate after

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. The dotFIT Vitamin D supplement is purposely only Vitamin D3 so that people can add to their dotFIT Multivitamin and Mineral Formula (MVM - all dotFIT MVMs contain 1000-1200 IUs [25-30 mcg] of Vitamin D) if and when they need more Vitamin D based on a blood test or time of year. The dotFIT MVMs all have between 80 and 100 mcg of Vitamin K and you would not want much more than that daily from a supplement (unless for clinical reasons). Our SuperCalcium also has a small amount of Vitamin K (30 mcg/tab) and Vitamin D in case someone is not using the MVM; the product will still be very effective. We make sure all products are synergistic to avoid someone crossing the Tolerable Upper Limit (UL) of any ingredient, so people will always be in a safe and effective range. See below for more information.
>
> Thanks again and have a great weekend!
>
> More Info
>
> Make sure your total vitamin D intake from all supplement sources are as follows: (see typical dotFIT user example)
>
> When blood tests are not available:
> - Up to 70 years of age: consume 1,000–1,500 IUs per day of vitamin D3 from supplements
> - Overweight adolescents and adults, persons >70 years, active adolescents, and adult athletes: consume 2,000 IUs per day of vitamin D3 from supplements
>
> When using blood tests:
> - Up to 70 years of age: serum 25(OH)D concentration of 75–100 nmol/L (30–40 ng/mL)
> - >70 years of age: serum 25(OH)D concentration of 100 nmol/L (~40 ng/mL)
> - Active adolescents and adult athletes: serum 25(OH)D concentration of 100–120 nmol/L (~40–50 ng/mL)
>
> The 70+ yrs and athletic recommendation for serum 25(OH)D concentration levels may likely require a minimum of 2,000 IUs per day (and often up to 4,000 IUs/d) of vitamin D3 supplementation and 1.5 times more in overweight individuals.
>
> Typical dotFIT user example reaching the serum 25(OH)D concentration of 75–100 nmol/L (30–40 ng/mL):
> - 1000–1200 IUs of VDS from multivitamin & mineral formula (Active MV, Women’s MV, or Over 50 MV)
> - 0–400 IUs of VDS from Calcium (Ca) supplement
>   - 0 (if not needed), 200 IUs (500 mg of calcium if needed) or 400 IUs (if 1,000 mg/d of calcium is needed)
> - 1,000 IUs from one (1) Vitamin D3 supplement
>
> Total VDS will always be between 2,000 and 2,600 IUs with this dosage being effective at reaching serum 25(OH)D concentration of 75–100 nmol/L (30–40 ng/mL).
>
> Typical Use dotFIT Vitamin D
>
> Note: 1.0 nmol/L = 0.4 ng/mL of 25-hydroxyvitamin D concentration (25(OH)D); 1 mcg D3 = 40 IUs
>
> When using blood tests: anyone seeking or advised to increase their vitamin D status, as determined by their serum 25(OH)D concentration, to a level of 75–100 nmol/L (30–40 ng/mL) to maximize vitamin D's contribution to the body’s structural development and tissue/organ maintenance throughout all phases of life. If below this standard level, start with one (1) soft gel daily and adjust as necessary.
>
> - Up to 70 years of age: serum 25(OH)D concentration of 75–100 nmol/L (30–40 ng/mL)
> - > 70 years old: serum 25(OH)D concentration of 100 nmol/L (~40 ng/mL)
> - Active adolescents and adult athletes: serum 25(OH)D concentration of 100–125 nmol/L (~40–50 ng/mL)
>
> Note: the >70 yr and athletic recommendation for serum 25(OH)D concentration levels may likely require a minimum of 2,000 IUs/d (and often up to 4,000 IUs/d) of vitamin D3 supplementation and 1.5 times more in overweight individuals.
>
> When blood tests are not available (unless advised otherwise by a qualified health professional):
> If using other supplements (e.g., MVM, calcium, etc.) containing a total of 0–1,000 IUs (25 mcg) of vitamin D, take one (1) dotFIT Vitamin D3 soft gel with any meal.
>
> - Up to 70 years: consume 1,000–1,500 IUs/d (25–37.5 mcg) of vitamin D3 from dotFIT supplements
> - Overweight adolescents and adults, persons >70 years, and active adolescents and adult athletes: consume 2,000 IUs/d (50 mcg) of vitamin D3 from supplements; therefore take one (1) to two (2) dotFIT Vitamin D3 soft gels.

> Use if you do not meet the daily needs of calcium (~1000-1200 mg) from food/shake intake.
>
> Females take 1 or 2 daily with meals; if you need to take 2, take one with AM meal and the 2nd with PM meal; males take 1 only if necessary.
>
> SuperOmega-3
>
> Take 1-2 daily with meal if not consuming 8-16 oz/week fatty fish (take 1 if >8 oz/week but <16 oz/week; 2 if ≤8 oz/week); not necessary if consuming ≥16 oz/week unless for athletic recovery purposes.
>
> Workout days
>
> FirstString* - use as directed to get:
> 25-35 gm protein 30-40 minutes before workout & repeat same dose immediately post exercise.
>
> *May substitute favorite dotFIT Nutrition High Protein Bar based on venue convenience.
>
> Meal Timings
>
> As possible eat every 4 hours.
>
> Large pre-training/event meal 2-3 hours before training.
>
> Large post meal ~30-60 minutes after last post-workout supplement.
>
> Early morning training
>
> Eat a large pre-training type meal the night before.
>
> Consume only the pre-workout snack/shake before training (as shown above) & follow workout day supplement schedule above.
>
> Tournament play (multiple games)
>
> <1.5 hour break: bars and hydration/electrolyte recovery drink.
>
> 1.5-2.5 hours: small pre-training-type meal.
>
> >2.5 hours: normal pre-training meal.
>
> Fluid Recommendations*
>
> 16 oz 2 hours before activity (extra 8-16 oz 1 hour before on hot days).
>
> 4-8 oz every 20 minutes during activity.
>
> 20 oz for every pound of weight loss post-activity.
>
> *Use electrolyte formula (e.g. Gatorade) and water as directed.

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. Although the dotFIT products will all test GMO free, we do not pay for the extra testing certification (unnecessary added cost to consumers), which is why you don't see it on labels (except for the Alln1 SuperBlendTM which is automatically certified). All our testing and certifications are on branded ingredients and 3rd and 4th party testing, which assures purity, potency, safety, and efficacy. These certifications are expensive but in high demand for our Practitioner channel including pro, college, Olympic sports and our licensed health and fitness facilities.
>
> dotFIT products are Practitioner Products and therefore meet far different standards than products sold in consumer channels (please see below for details on Practitioner Product status).
>
> Have a great day!
>
> 3rd Party Tested & NSF Certified for Sport Dietary Supplements
> dotFIT is Evidence Based* 24/7 Support with Practitioner Products Only
> dotFIT Worldwide is R&D company that delivers holistic evidence-based programming, products and support to over 1500 Sport/Fitness & Health Facilities, and over 40,000 sport & fitness professionals
> #1 Recommended and Used Dietary Supplements in the Sport & Fitness Channels including college and professional sports
>
> If you are looking for health, performance and fitness support with Practitioner Products only (not found in mass consumer channels and sold through professionals) and want full product disclosure (rationale for use, mechanisms of action, safety, contraindications, etc.) you can click the link to the dotFIT Practitioner's Dietary Supplement Reference Guide (PDSRG) that is used by all our sport, fitness and health professionals and published by our Scientific Advisory Board and R&D team. The first section describes everything around criteria, recommendation, testing and manufacturing processes. All products are 3rd party tested and specific dotFIT health and performance products are also NSF Certified for Sport (NSFCS). Peer review quality documents for individual products are found in their respective categories. dotFIT is a research and development company for sport, fitness, and health professionals, therefore, all programs/products must pass our Evidence-Based Advisory and Legal Committee Rules including 3rd party testing procedures such as Health Canada, NSF and mandatory audited GMPs. Individual products can be found in respective sections. PDSRG (used for formulation safety and efficacy rationale, certification and education purposes, not consumer facing and located in the certified practitioner console).
>
> *Evidence based: sport and fitness/weight control solutions (or clinical decisions) that represent the best available evidence and supported by the consensus of their respective scientific community
>
> Practitioner Product Status by Rules
>
> Efficacy – dosages & forms match clinical trials (peer review documents published in PDSRG)
> Safety – shown in trials and history, screening & ingredient synergy
> Purity & Potency – tested from start to finish (GMPs)
> Nutrient Delivery – right place, right time
> Truth in labeling – legal facts based on #1 & prove it with 3rd party testing
> Practitioner delivered – Personalized Practitioner Delivery (legitimizes the recommendation): synergistic solution based on body statistics, sport, goals, experience, medical history & progress
>
> Remove the doubt with the Practitioner resource
> Click here Sports (3:23); all consumers (6min)
>
> Takeaway
> You need both: 1) documentation that doses/forms match positive clinical trials found in the practitioner dietary supplement reference guide (PDSRG) i.e., submitted documentation
> and 2) 3rd party testing to make sure it’s in the product at that dosage (Ref)

Further, your client should be following a diet recommended for this condition, such as:
> Eat foods low in fat and fiber.
> Eat five or six small, nutritious meals a day instead of two or three large meals.
> Chew your food thoroughly.
> Eat soft, well-cooked foods.
> Avoid carbonated or fizzy beverages.
> Avoid alcohol.
> Hope this helps –

> Hi [NAME],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. We had a LeanMR flavor enhancement a few months ago but no active ingredient formula change, so there should not be any alterations in consistency or results unless you are mixing it differently. That said, using natural ingredients always makes a minor change in appearance and taste based on harvest season differences. The latest lots of LeanMR are our best to date according to our focus groups and sales, but if for any reason you would like to exchange it or receive a refund, [NAME] is here to help. And yes, you can use the Pre/Post Workout and Meal Replacement Formula (PPWMR) as a meal replacement as it is, or add ingredients to make it as you like. Visit our recipe section for great recipes. I also placed a helpful Q&A below for more information.
>
> Thanks again and have a great Sunday — and reach out to [NAME] as needed.
>
> Q: Right now I'm taking LeanMR, and I noticed you have a pre/post protein powder. Which one is better for a workout and lose weight? Thanks
> A: The LeanMR is a specialized structured meal replacement (MR) that you would generally not use for a pre and post workout supplement because of the high fiber. The Pre/Post Workout and Meal Replacement Formula (PPWMR) can be used both as a MR and as your pre/post workout supplement (also see recipe section). Mindful that the great part of protein powders is that you can add desired extra nutrition to get the calories, fiber, and macros that you want. The LeanMR starting formula has 6gms of fiber while the PPWMR has virtually none, but you can add as desired, therefore it can be used for both purposes. If you choose to use the LeanMR for managing bodyfat/weight, then you would use the AminoFormula for your pre/post workout supplement. We also placed below a brief description of all dotFIT protein powders. Feel free to contact [NAME] (cc’d here) to talk to a live coach for more information.

> Hi [NAME],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. We do not make other flavors of ExtremeCreatineXXXL+ (ECX) or CreatineMonohydrate (CM). Many people will put CM and/or ECX in their smoothies or pre/post workout formulas such as the dotFIT proteins, AminoFormula, etc., for different flavors, which is also why we keep a relatively neutral flavor for our CM/ECX. See below for a related FAQ and more information. Further, our licensed facilities have limited room on their shelves and can only carry a small number of SKUs.
>
> Thanks again and have a great day.
>
> Q: Will you make more flavors of ExtremeCreatineXXXL+?
> A: We probably won’t make other flavor extensions of ExtremeCreatineXXXL+ (ECX) in the near future primarily because the demand is not high enough to justify a full run including the NSF-CS testing and our facilities only have so much shelf space for supplements so we must pick our battles. Most people do mix it in their shakes including adding it in many different flavored smoothies. Further, because the flavor is mild it complements other products when mixed with them, such as the different flavors of the AminoFormula, which is commonly mixed with ECX.
>
> More Info
>
> We do not make another flavored ExtremeCreatineXXXL+ (ECX) or CreatineMonohydrate because most of our facilities can only carry so much inventory (see below for more on making product flavor extensions or other niche products). ECX is purposely the current flavor so that it blends with any of the pre and post proteins and the different flavored Amino Formulas, since most people will mix it with those powders. The mild raspberry flavor also allows it to be mixed with juices of preference if you would like to alter the taste.
>
> The only reason we don't come out with products the way everyone would want them produced (and there are many requests), would be because at this point in time within our sport/fitness practitioner product distribution channel, we don't have a large enough respective market to produce a niche product/flavor in the quantities necessary to be even slightly competitive with currently available specialty product lines that cater to unique markets. You must produce 10s of thousands at one time of each product to be price competitive as we do for all other products we sell with the additional cost of 3rd party/NSF testing. Doing this with a unique requested particular product/flavor, because our market for that specialty product or flavor is limited, most of the product would expire before we sold it, leaving a huge waste and lost revenues.
>
> When the market increases for other flavors, sizes or particular products driven by our practitioner distribution channel, we will add them as necessary. Moreover, our licensed outlets only have so much room for inventory making them unable to carry many different flavors/sizes of the same product and therefore only carry top sellers.

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. The quick answer is yes you can add your CreatineMonohydrate (CM) to your NO7Rage drink. But since NO7 has 5gms of creatine monohydrate in the typical 2-scoop dose, we would rather have you take your separate CM at another time such as after your workout with your post workout shake or meal to maximize daily creatine levels. See below for a complete program that is common to professional strength and performance athletes where both products are used. Ignore the loading phase since you have been already using the product and follow the maintenance instructions. Because you are 190LBS you will use 10gms of creatine daily between both sources.
>
> Thanks again and have a great weekend.
>
> Adult Performance & Size Supplement Plan with Creatine Monohydrate & NO7 Rage (maintain BA levels)
>
> Active Multivitamin Mineral Formula
> Take two (2) daily: one immediately following first large meal and one following the final meal of the day
>
> SuperOmega-3
> Take 1-2 daily with meal if not consuming 8-16oz/wk fatty fish (take 1 if >8oz/wk but <16; 2 if 6396;8oz/wk); not necessary if consuming 6396;16oz/wk unless for athletic recovery purposes
>
> FirstString (or WheySmooth depending on daily calorie allotment)
> Take two (2) scoops 30-45 minutes before workout or use favorite dotFIT AE bar for convenience
> Take two (2) scoops 20-30 minutes after post-workout AminoFormula dose
> Use anytime throughout the day as a supplement to a meal or by itself mixed with desired ingredients to add protein/calories as needed to meet daily needs.
> Be sure to ingest ~1 gram of protein per pound of LBM (or weight if not overweight) daily from all sources including food. Ideally, divide protein into 4-5 meals/day along with pre/post shakes (the pre/post combined generally supply 50-80 grams of daily protein goal)
>
> AminoFormula (workout days only)
> Take 1-1.5 scoops (based on weight) approximately 10 minutes before workout (add to NO7Rage if using this product). You may continue to drink during workout.
> Take one (1) scoop immediately following workout.
> Begin Creatine Monohydrate loading at 2nd week of training.
>
> CreatineMonohydrate Loading & Maintenance Strategy (if you are under 175LBS you can cut the daily maintenance dose in half* - i.e., after loading period, take a total of 5gms daily and split as described below)
> Mix 1 scoop (5gms) with 4-8oz of favorite fluid/shake and take 4 times daily with a protein and/or carbohydrate containing meal/drink for first 5 days. Thereafter take 1 scoop twice daily to maintain stores. To help maximize creatine uptake, split doses throughout the day with meals/drinks containing protein and/or carbohydrate.
> Training days: take 1 dose before workout & 1 after with meals/drinks (after loading phase, may discontinue pre-workout dose because NO7 will supply Cr). May mix with pre/post training formula.
> Non-training days* take 1 dose with AM meal and 1 dose with PM meal.
> *Program totals daily creatine first 5 days: 20gms/d. Maintenance phase: all days 5-10gms. To maximize uptake, creatine intake should be spread evenly as possible throughout the day with some protein and/or carb containing meals/shakes.
>
> Dosing by body weight:
> Loading phase 0.14 g/LB/d split into four (4) daily intakes of 5 g each, followed by a maintenance phase dose of 0.04 g/LB/d for the duration of the supplementation period.
>
> NO7Rage3
> Daily (to maintain BA & Cr levels) take 1.5 to 2.5 scoops (depending on body weight). If necessary, because of caffeine sensitivity, start with lower dose and increase to proper body weight dosage if not uncomfortably affected by caffeine. Take approximately 10 minutes before workout (may mix with AminoFormula and continue to consume during workout). See directions on label for weight dosages.
>
> NO7Rage contains 150mg of caffeine per scoop. As a reference: Starbucks Grande-drip coffee contains ~330 mg of caffeine.
>
> Non-training days, take 1 serving anytime of the day but do not use within 4 hours of sleep.
>
> *Programs total daily creatine first 5 days: 20g/d (plus 3.7-6.5 g from NO7 on training days). Maintenance phase: workout days = 7.5-12.5; non-workout days 7.5-12.5g. To maximize uptake, creatine intake should be spread as evenly as possible throughout the day and around the workout as described and ingested with some carb and/or protein containing meals or shakes. BA total daily intake = 3-5gm.

>

> - Large post meal ~30-60min after last post workout supplement
>
> Early morning training
> - Eat a large pre-training type meal the night before
> - Consume only the pre-workout snack/shake before training (as shown above) & follow workout day supplement schedule above
>
> Tournament play (multiple games)
> - <1.5Hr break: bars and hydration/electrolyte recovery drink
> - 1.5-2.5Hr: small pre-training-type meal
> - >2.5Hr: normal pre-training meal
>
> Fluid Recommendations*
> - 16 oz 2Hr before activity (extra 8-16 oz 1hr before on hot days)
> - 4-8 oz every 20 minutes during activity
> - 20 oz for every pound of weight loss post-activity
>
> *Use electrolyte formula (e.g. Gatorade) and water as directed

>
> Meal Replacement Integration
> Weight loss phase:
> Meal Replacements (MRs) generally supply two small meals a day and allow freedom of choice from traditional foods for the remaining allotted foods/calories. Therefore, use as two small meals within any calorie restricted meal plan of 4-5 meals since it's been shown that frequent small meals are better for weight loss than fewer larger ones especially as it relates to satiety, preservation of lean body mass (LBM) and energy levels.
>

> Maintenance phase
> Continue to consume 4-5 small meals daily within caloric allotment that include 2 MRs for convenience and to help ensure overall diet quality while reducing food costs.

**Source answer:**

> Hi [NAME], Thanks for contacting us. You can take the supplements in the Lean Pack 90 as directed over the 90-day period, meaning each supplement for 30 days, or you can take them all together. You can use the products as they are designed for the kit, meaning using the Weight Loss and Liver Support the first 30 days; CarbRepel the second 30 days; and ThermAccel for the last 30 days for a total of 90 days. Or you can use them simultaneously if you need extra assistance or have a tight timeline to lose weight since they all work by different mechanisms. Simply use each one as directed daily. Yes, you can use CarbRepel with ThermAccel (TA), or any of the dotFIT weight loss products such as Weight Loss and Liver Support together, and it is commonly done with people needing extra help, as each product works to support appetite and metabolism through different and unique mechanisms. Simply follow the directions on each product label. You would not use CarbRepel if not consuming carbs, or very little carbs such as only vegetables or a little fruit. Taking CR as it was used in the clinical trials is ideal (~30 minutes before a carb-containing meal), but it will still work if you take it at/before, or very close to, the meal. The Weight Loss & Liver Support should be consumed three times daily and separated as evenly as possible to help maintain the desired levels of ingredients. Taking it before a meal is one way of spreading it out and getting help in appetite support within 30 minutes, but it’s not necessary once you start using the product regularly. And yes, have your client take it in the morning even if not consuming breakfast. Rationale for cycling: liver support first when at heaviest; different appetite support/targets to overcome adaptation; most aggressive product (ThermAccel) at final phase as natural plateaus are more common. Marketing rationale for the kit includes a psychological timeline and a financially palatable structure to encourage full commitment. Thanks again and have a great day.

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. Yes, your product is still safe and effective well past the expiration date. See below for more information.
>
> Thanks again and have a great day.
>
> More Info
> The “Expiration Dates” are basically a “best sold by date” with the product still potent. All dates are 2 years after production, only because you don’t need much more than that based on inventory turnover. But the product is still fine (safe and effective) many months to at least 2 years after the date in case you have not used it in a while, and kept it sealed.
>
> The definition of an expiration date for nutritional products such as ours, is that the product must be within 10% of label claim of each ingredient on the last day, meaning it’s still at declared potency 2 years following production (only because they were tested at that milestone and all is still perfect, and generally no one carries more than a few months of inventory and the next lot has a new date that starts all over). Therefore, our pills and powders have a 2 yr expiration, and still safe and effective to use well after the date because the only issue might be that they are a little less potent than when manufactured, but that is something that would only happen well after the published date. In the case of expired nutrition bars, the product is still safe and effective after the date but not as fresh tasting and therefore we recommend you replace it with a new lot because taste matters.
>

But people may have older non-used jugs at home, or on a shelf, and as long as it was sealed so nothing strange entered it, it will be fine. See below for more on expiration dates.

All said, no matter what supplements you choose, make sure you clear all with your doctor.

>
> Thanks for contacting us. We have all seen the studies and this is not the first time plant proteins have been associated with naturally occurring metals/minerals or environmental chemicals, such as Bisphenol A (BPA), at levels of the government’s upper limits or beyond. Since plant proteins originate in soil, it is a more complicated process to remove the unwanted minerals/chemicals or reduce them to allowable limits, so many manufacturers do an incomplete job mainly because of costs. The good news is that the dotFIT BestPlantProtein (like all dotFIT products) always test below allowable limits because it is made in an NSF Certified facility and therefore regularly audited. >

If you want more information including a C of A (Certificate of Analysis), I’m connecting you with [NAME] (VP of Product Development) and if you would like, he can put you in direct contact with the science staff at Reliance.
>
> All of our products must test for heavy metals and specific environmental chemicals and the manufacturers maintain records. > Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. None of the dotFIT protein powders contain soy protein. The only fraction of soy used is soy lecithin, which is not from the protein. It is contained in most food/ingredient powders in minute amounts as a mixing agent.
>
> We included a related FAQ below. However, if for some unusual reason you can’t have soy lecithin, you can use BestPlantProtein or All Natural WheySmooth protein powders because they do not use soy lecithin. But again, our powders do not contain soy protein. See below for more information.
>
> Thanks again and reach out to [NAME] to help.
>
> More info
>
> Soy listed in powders is soy lecithin, not the protein
>
> Q: I notice some of your protein powders say, “contains soy [lecithin]”?
> A: There is no soy protein in any of our protein powders. Only soy lecithin in minute amounts used as an emulsifier (enhances mixing properties including supporting ingredient uniformity), like most powders of any kind sold everywhere. It is not allergenic to the majority of people with soy allergies because it’s not from soy protein, it’s from the fat.43,44 (from FAQ on the website)
>
> More info:
>
> Soy lecithin is an FDA approved food additive for proper emulsification used in a variety of mixes. Soy lecithin is used in most mixes of any kind in minute amounts as you can tell from its appearance in the “other ingredients” listings or no listing other than “contains soy” with no amounts listed. Don’t mix up soy lecithin with soy protein – there is no connection, soy lecithin is a mixing agent not from the protein portion of soy so can’t trigger normal soy allergies. Many foods contain soy lecithin and most good protein powders, and it’s a very safe and effective emulsifier. 43,44

> Typical Use
> Non-stimulant bodyfat-loss aid
> Recommended for overweight people to support a complete weight loss program including related liver health
> Take one tablet, three times daily, generally 30 minutes before meals with at least 8 fl. oz of water, or spread 3 tablets evenly throughout your awake hours.
> Discontinue after reaching fat loss goal.
>
> The bottom line is that WLLS should be consumed 3 times daily and separated as evenly as possible to help maintain the desired levels of ingredients regardless of total daily meals or frequency. Taking it before a meal is one way of spreading it out and getting extra help in appetite support within 30 minutes. >
> Hi [NAME],
> Thanks for contacting us. The bottom line with Weight Loss & Liver Support (WLLS) is that one dose (pill) needs to be taken 3 times daily spread as evenly as possible throughout the day and this will maintain the ingredients' effectiveness. Taking it before meals is more a reminder and probably slightly better for the African Mango appetite support. Again, just make sure to take 1 dose 3 times daily spread as evenly as possible throughout your awake hours and you will be fine.
>
> See below for a related FAQ
> Q: A client asked me if she should still take WLLS in the morning if she's not going to eat breakfast. Also, what if you forget to take it 30 minutes before a meal? Can you take it right before or should you wait till later?
> A: The bottom line is that the Weight Loss & Liver Support should be consumed 3 times daily and separated as evenly as possible to help maintain the desired levels of ingredients. Taking it before a meal is one way of spreading it out and getting help in appetite support within 30 minutes. But it’s not necessary once you start using the product 3 times daily regularly, as proper blood levels will be maintained. And yes, have your client take it in the morning even if not consuming breakfast.

- (item-specific) …

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Everyone in the supplement business is trying to tie a little science on something that naturally occurs in the body that, when added, can produce the same results as something big like GLP-1 drugs. Unfortunately, that is not happening at this point in time (when it does, we will be the first to know). See below for how someone would connect the AMPK protein/enzyme to weight loss, mindful GLP-1 drugs suppress appetite leading to voluntary weight loss. AMPK is activated when cellular energy is low to help mobilize glucose, but adding exogenous sources will do little if anything to shut down the appetite compared to the drugs.
>
> Have a great Memorial Day weekend!
>
> AMP-Activated Protein Kinase (AMPK)
>
> AMP-activated protein kinase plays a role in cellular energy homeostasis, mostly to activate glucose and fatty acid uptake and oxidation when cellular energy is low. Net effect of AMPK activation is stimulation of hepatic fatty acid oxidation, ketogenesis, stimulation of skeletal muscle fatty acid oxidation and glucose uptake, inhibition of cholesterol synthesis, lipogenesis, and triglyceride synthesis, inhibition of adipocyte lipogenesis, inhibition of adipocyte lipolysis, and modulation of insulin secretion by pancreatic β-cells.

> Thanks for contacting us, and congrats on the progress. It is very simple to stay on track using the dotFIT program. When you plug in your weekly weight or biweekly body fat measurement, the program automatically tells you what to do. Or you can stay ahead of the response by going back to your goal page and inserting your new body weight and/or body fat and goal. The program will alter your daily calorie intake appropriately.
>
> I placed more information below that gives you an idea how everything works so that you would never come to a plateau until you want to — i.e., you reach your body composition goal.
>
> Thanks again and have a great Sunday.
>
> More information:
>
> This will give you the information you need to handle any plateau problem. It is how the dotFIT program works to continue losing body fat as you measure the client on weekly or bi-weekly measurement days.
>
> Diet Monitoring & Adjustments for Bodyfat Reduction
>
> Program Algorithms
>
> Adjustments:
> Measurable or visual reduction in body fat and/or weight proceeds in a consistent manner, e.g., a decrease in circumference inches and/or the desired average decrease in body fat per week.
>
> If progress stops or slows dramatically, use one or a combination of adjustments to re-start the process:
>
> - Increase daily activities (e.g., daily steps or other non-athletic/exercise activities).
> - Standing and pacing burns 1.5–2 times more calories than sitting for the same period.
> - There are approximately 2,000–2,500 steps (depending on stride length) in a mile. Walking 2,000 steps will burn ~75–150 more calories (depending on individual size) than sitting for the same time, takes ~20–30 minutes, and can be done anywhere, even in the office, while on the phone, or watching TV.
> - Increase cardio workout time and/or intensity and include interval training if necessary. Ex: if doing 30 minutes 5D/WK, go to 45 min and add interval work within the time frame.
> - Decrease food intake approximately 200 to 300 calories per day or remove a small portion (carbs/fats) of the largest meal other than the pre-workout meal (incorporate 3 days low/1 high as necessary).
> - Add dietary support beyond baseline as necessary (thermogenic, energy partitioning, appetite control, etc.).
>
> Repeat the process any time body fat is stable for at least one week. Always remember if you are supplementing properly and you stop losing fat, you need to eat less, move/burn more or a combination of the two regardless of what you read or hear from others. As you lower calorie intake, make sure your protein intake stays at or above 1 g per pound of body weight to protect LBM.

**Source answer:**

> Hey [CUSTOMER],
>
> Sodium is not listed because it’s not in a high enough amount that it needs to be listed*, which is why you see it only in “other ingredients” and listed last (lowest level of anything in the product). This also allows the “no salt” note/claim at the bottom. The Pink Himalayan Salt shown is only part of the flavoring package and exists in an insignificant amount (also contains other minerals/trace elements).
>
> Hope all is well my friend – keep up the good work!
>
> * According to FDA regulations, a product can contain less than 5 milligrams of sodium per serving and be labeled as "sodium-free" or "salt-free," meaning it does not need to list the sodium content on the label; this is the maximum amount of salt a product can have without needing to list sodium.

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. Sounds like you have stumbled into the junk internet science around seed oils (using animal studies with supraphysiological/unrealistic dosages). The minuscule amount of canola or other seed oils that a few of our products use is not just good for product production but can also be part of a healthy diet.
>
> Like anything else in your diet, seed oils are fine in moderation (see true science slide below). They should be part of an overall balanced eating plan that includes lots of vegetables, fruits, whole grains, and lean protein options.
>
> Of course, you can get too much of a good thing. The overuse of seed oils is the most problematic when they are an ingredient in ultra-processed foods. For example, if you eat a lot of deep-fried foods, pastries, crunchy fried snacks (such as chips) and donuts, you can get too much oil. These ultra-processed foods are also high in sugar, salt, and/or food additives, which are also partly to blame for the poor health outcomes.
>
> Here is the latest umbrella review on vegetable/seed oils, Health Effects of Various Edible Vegetable Oil: An Umbrella Review (Advance Nutrition: 2024 Jul 23;15(9):100276. doi: 10.1016/j.advnut.2024.100276)
>
> Thanks again and have a great day.

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. Always do your best (without pressure) to get your child to eat healthy. But we all know kids are picky eaters and no diet will supply all the nutrition they need to grow and develop to their full potential. Therefore, just as we did while the child was in the womb, we continue to fill their essential nutrition gaps to maximize growth and development, including height. For your 5 and 7-year-olds, a perfect daily approach is a Multivitamin and Mineral Formula (MVM) plus a great-tasting protein/carbohydrate mix like FirstString used to function as a delivery system for things kids don’t necessarily like but need (i.e., using a protein-based smoothie to include fruits, milk, veggies, etc.). It’s a win/win.
>
> See below and keep in mind all programs we create not only help prevent injury, speed recovery, and improve training/exercise-induced results but also contribute to better overall health while maximizing growth and development potential including height. Proper nutrition makes our children and athletes better “on the field” and in the classroom.
>
> The FirstString protein mix/shake is a 3rd-party tested and lactose-free (as all our products) food product that we have the youth athletes (any active youth) we work with use for a pre- and post-activity shake for energy and recovery, and/or weight/muscle gain. Below is more information including incorporating FirstString into daily planning along with a multivitamin and mineral formula (MVM) to also aid recovery and fill important nutrient gaps common to youngsters. If a child has diet restrictions on animal protein, they would use the Best Plant Protein – Vegan product. I have also cc’d [NAME] here in case you have any questions.
>
> Thanks again and have a great night.
>
> Youth/Athlete Growth, Development, Performance, Recovery & Health Dietary Support Plan
>
> Click on product links for a brief product description including short video
>
> Daily:
> KidMV (ages 2–11 yrs): 2–4 yrs take 1; 5–11 yrs take 2; Ages 12–17 yrs use ActiveMV and Take-1 daily with a meal until age 18; then take 2 daily, one with AM meal and one with PM meal.
>
> Daily as needed:
> FirstString
> Use as directed to make sure you get ~1 gm of protein per pound of LBM (or weight if not overweight) daily from all sources including foods divided 4–5 times daily, which include the pre/post activity shake.
> Can be used as a supplement to a meal or by itself (or mixed with desired supporting contents) to shore up total daily protein and calorie needs for weight/muscle gain needs.
>
> SuperCalcium (read all food and shake labels) (using FirstString or other dotFIT protein powder regularly with other typical diet calcium intake would probably meet calcium needs without supplementing)
> Use if you do not meet the daily needs of calcium (~1000–1200 mgs) from food/shake intake.
> Females take 1 or 2 daily with meals; if you need to take 2, take one with AM meal and the 2nd with PM meal; Males take 1 only if necessary.
>
> SuperOmega-3
> Take 1–2 daily with a meal if not consuming 8–16 oz/wk fatty fish (take 1 if >8 oz/wk but <16; 2 if ≤8 oz); not necessary if consuming ≥16 oz/wk unless for athletic recovery purposes.
>
> Workout days
> FirstString* - use as directed to get:
> 21 gm protein (2 scoops) 30–40 min before workout & repeat same dose immediately post exercise
>
> *May substitute favorite dotFIT Nutrition High Protein Bar based on venue convenience

>
> Weight loss phase:
> Meal Replacements (MRs) generally supply two small meals a day and allow freedom of choice from traditional foods for the remaining allotted foods/calories. Therefore, use as two small meals within any calorie restricted meal plan of 4-5 meals since it's been shown that frequent small meals are better for weight loss than fewer larger ones especially as it relates to satiety, preservation of lean body mass (LBM) and energy levels
> Get LeanMR recipes here: https://www.dotfit.com/category-cid-1868.html
>
> Maintenance phase
> Continue to consume 4-5 small meals daily within caloric allotment that include 2 MRs for convenience and to help ensure overall diet quality while reducing food costs.
>
> You can go to our recipe section on the dotFIT website to get recipes with meal preparation instructions including meal replacement recipes.
