> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> BrainHealth is a preventative formula that delivers the nutrients the brain needs as we age, but no longer can acquire these nutrients in the proper amounts. It certainly would not hurt your coach, but in the bigger picture I would include a daily 5 gm dose of CreatineMonohydrate as it is commonly used to help heal concussed athletes. Therefore, all in all, if it fits his budget, I would have him use the program below to support his brain functioning and overall health. He can use the ActiveMV instead of the Alln1 SuperBlendTM if he has a budget concern, but the Alln1 SuperBlendTM also contains the extra dotFIT health products beyond the multivitamin, such as the SuperiorAntioxidant, UltraProbiotic, Digestive Enzymes, along with the herbs that also support brain function (especially the mushroom blend).
>
> Keep me updated on his progress and have him see his doctor regularly for the proper medical advice.
>
> Thanks.
>
> Daily:
> Alln1 SuperBlendTM (SB)
> Take as directed: (1st 2 weeks ½ serving daily; 3rd week full serving daily; may take all at once or ½ in AM and ½ in PM; visit recipe section to incorporate as desired into daily lifestyle*)
> Vitamin D (to achieve serum 25(OH)D concentration of 40-50 ng/ml)
> Take daily with SB or anytime
>
> CreatineMonohydrate Loading & Maintenance Strategy
> Mix 1 scoop (5 gms) with 4-8 oz of favorite fluid/shake and take 4 times daily with a protein and/or carbohydrate-containing meal/drink for first 5 days. Thereafter take 1 scoop once daily to maintain stores. To help maximize creatine uptake, split doses throughout the day with meals/drinks containing protein and/or carbohydrate.
> Training days: take 1 scoop before or after your workout with meals/drinks. May mix with your pre & post training formula.
> Non-training days: take 1 scoop with a meal or shake.
>
> Daily as needed:
> Favorite dotFIT protein mix (WheySmooth, All Natural WheySmooth and the all vegan BestPlantProtein are most popular and cover all diet types – all are lactose free)1
> Use as directed to make sure you get ~1 gm of protein per pound of LBM (or weight if not overweight) daily from all sources including foods divided 3-5 times daily.
> If exercising, use as directed to get ~25-35 gm protein 30-40 min before workout & repeat dose immediately post workout (see below).
> Can be used as a supplement to a meal or by itself (or mixed with desired supporting contents) to shore up total daily protein needs.
>
> SuperOmega-3
> Take 1-2 daily with meal if not consuming 8-16 oz/wk fatty fish (take 1 if >8 oz/wk but <16; 2 if ≤8 oz/wk); not necessary if consuming ≥16 oz/wk unless for athletic recovery purposes.
>
> Advanced Brain Health (may divide evenly or all at once anytime with or without meals)
> 45-55 yrs take 4; 56+ take 8
>
> Workout days
> dotFIT Protein of choice. Use as directed to get: 25-35 gm protein 30-40 min before workout & repeat same dose immediately following exercise.
>
> 1 May substitute favorite dotFIT Nutrition High Protein Bar based on venue convenience.
>
> * Most popular way of consuming SB daily is mixing it with water and 25 gms of vanilla WheySmooth.
>
> Nutrition facts based on 1 scoop of WS and 1 scoop of Alln1 SuperBlendTM:
> 25 gms protein, 16.5 gm CHO, 4 gms fat, 352 mg calcium, 224 mg potassium, 195 cals.
>
> Full days’ recommendation for: vitamins and minerals, antioxidants, probiotics, prebiotics, Ashwagandha, turmeric & mushroom blend, along with 6 gms of fiber and 2 full servings of vegetables.
>
> As desired, add any fruit for the perfect one and done health habit.

> Hi [CUSTOMER],

> Q: I noticed that my dotFIT vitamins often look different and some have a stronger smell than others. Is this normal?
> A: Yes, all dotFIT multivitamin and mineral formulas are slightly different in looks, primarily because of the choline addition and new enteric coating. To a lesser degree on coloring and smell, all multiple vitamin and mineral (MVM) products that contain natural ingredients such as ours (and don’t use artificial coatings) will have changing distinct features depending on ingredient harvesting and seasons including colors and even smell just like the plants they come from in nature. All our MVMs also use a delayed release feature so that your body absorbs and uses the ingredients more efficiently and we recently upgraded this, which also altered the coloring. At the end of the day, because our dietary supplements are in the 3rd party tested Practitioner product category, everything on the label is accurate no matter the color or taste and they follow the science - i.e., continually updated.

> Youth/Athlete Growth, Development, Performance, Recovery & Health Dietary Support Plan
>
> Click on product links for a brief product description including short video
>
> Keep in mind all programs we create not only help prevent injury, speed recovery, improve training/exercise induced results but also contribute to better overall health while maximizing growth and development potential. Proper nutrition makes our children and athletes better “on the field” and in the classroom.
>
> The FirstString Protein mix/shake is a 3rd party tested and lactose free (as all our products) food product that we have the youth athletes (any active youth) we work with use for a pre and post activity shake for energy and recovery, and/or weight/muscle gain. Below is more information including incorporating FirstString into daily planning along with a multivitamin and mineral formula (MVM) to also aid recovery and fill important nutrient gaps common to youngsters. (If child has diet restrictions on animal protein, use the Best Plant Protein – Vegan product).
>
> Daily:
> Ages 12-17 use ActiveMV and Take-1 daily with meal until age 18; then take 2 daily, one with AM meal and one with PM meal
>
> Daily as needed:
> FirstString
> Use as directed to make sure you get ~1 gm of protein per pound of LBM (or weight if not overweight) daily from all sources including foods divided 5 times daily
> Can be used as a supplement to a meal or by itself (or mixed with desired supporting contents) to shore up total daily protein and calorie needs for weight/muscle gain needs
>
> SuperCalcium (read all food and shake labels) (using FirstString or other dotFIT protein powder regularly with other typical diet calcium intake would probably meet calcium needs without supplementing)
> Use if you do not meet the daily needs of calcium (~1000-1200 mgs) from food/shake intake
> Females take 1 or 2 daily with meals; if you need to take 2, take one with AM meal and the 2nd with PM meal; Males take 1 only if necessary
>
> SuperOmega-3
> Take 1-2 daily with meal if not consuming 8-16 oz/wk fatty fish (take 1 if >8 oz/wk but <16; 2 if ≤8 oz/wk); not necessary if consuming ≥16 oz/wk unless for athletic recovery purposes
>
> Workout days
> FirstString* - use as directed to get:
> 25-35 gm protein 30-40 min before workout & repeat same dose immediately post exercise
> *May substitute favorite dotFIT Nutrition High Protein Bar based on venue convenience
>
> Meal Timings
> As possible eat every 4 hours
> Large pre-training/event meal 2-3 hr before training
> Large post meal ~30-60 min after last post workout supplement
>
> Early morning training
> Eat a large pre-training type meal the night before
> Consume only the pre-workout snack/shake before training (as shown above) & follow workout day supplement schedule above
>
> Tournament play (multiple games)
> <1.5 hr break: bars and hydration/electrolyte recovery drink
> 1.5-2.5 hr: small pre-training-type meal
> >2.5 hr: normal pre-training meal
>
> Fluid Recommendations*
> 16 oz 2 hr before activity (extra 8-16 oz 1 hr before on hot days)
> 4-8 oz every 20 minutes during activity
> 20 oz for every pound of weight loss post-activity
>
> *Use electrolyte formula (e.g. Gatorade) and water as directed

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Always great to see you are still at it! Yes, add creatine to your program. Just put it in your shake or with a meal anytime so that you get better absorption. 5 gms daily will be enough, unless you want to load it first to get an earlier ergogenic effect. Creatine supplementation is especially important to older exercisers that want to remain active.
>
> I put a program below that is common for older exercisers. Make sure you watch the new podcast, SuppBeast (see slide at the bottom of the email). You will love it, and I believe the new creatine segment is getting released now.
>
> Have a great Thanksgiving!
>
> General Sport Health, Activity Recovery, Performance & Muscle Gain/Maintenance Program - CM In Baseline/AF (no protein stacking)
>
> Click on product links for a brief product description including short video
>
> Daily:
>
> dotFIT Multivitamin & Mineral for your age and gender. If 50 yrs or older use Over50 unless noted below; if female under 50 use Women’s; males and very active females (competitive training protocols) under 50 use 2-Active, with both genders continuing use till 65 yrs if still training competitively at which time every person will use Over50; children under 12 yr use Kids; children 12-17 yr use 1-Active.
>
> Take as directed with meals.
>
> CreatineMonohydrate Loading & Maintenance Strategy (if you are under 175 LBS you can cut the daily maintenance dose in half* — i.e., after loading period, take a total of 5 gms daily and split as described below)
>
> Mix 1 scoop (5 gms) with 4–8 oz of favorite fluid/shake and take 4 times daily with a protein and/or carbohydrate-containing meal/drink for first 5 days. Thereafter take 1 scoop twice daily to maintain stores. To help maximize creatine uptake, split doses throughout the day with meals/drinks containing protein and/or carbohydrate.
>
> Training days: take 1 dose before workout and 1 after with meals/drinks. May mix with your pre & post training formula.
>
> Non-training days*: take 1 dose with AM meal and 1 dose with PM meal.
>
> *Program total daily creatine first 5 days: 20 gms/day. Maintenance phase: all days 5–10 gms. To maximize uptake, creatine intake should be spread as evenly as possible throughout the day with some protein and/or carb-containing meals or shakes.
>
> Daily as needed:
>
> FirstString or other favorite dotFIT protein mix (based on caloric allotment for goal)
>
> Use as directed to make sure you get ~1 gm of protein per pound of LBM (or weight if not overweight) daily from all sources including foods divided 3–5 times daily.
>
> Can be used as a supplement to a meal or by itself (or mixed with desired supporting contents) to shore up total daily protein needs.
>
> Super Calcium (read all food labels for calcium content)
>
> Use if you do not meet the daily needs of calcium (~1000–1200 mg) from food/shake intake.
>
> Females take 1 or 2 daily with meals; if you need to take 2, take one with AM meal and the second with PM meal; males take 1 only if necessary.
>
> Super Omega 3
>
> Take 1–2 daily with meal if not consuming 8–16 oz/week fatty fish (take 1 if >8 oz/wk but <16; 2 if ≤8 oz); not necessary if consuming ≥16 oz/wk unless for athletic recovery purposes, then increase as directed.
>
> Workout days
>
> AminoFormula
>
> Take 1 scoop ~5–10 min before and may continue to drink during workout.
>
> Take 1 scoop immediately following workout.
>
> Or use both scoops and start ingesting 10 min before workout and consume at a pace so it’s finished at end of activity.
>
> Creatine Monohydrate
>
> On training days, use one dose before workout and one after with meals/drinks. May mix with your pre & post training formula.

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. Collagen protein is not a complete protein (lacks tryptophan and cysteine and is low in essential amino acids (EAA) necessary for muscle protein synthesis) and therefore is not used in total muscle recovery. **Source answer:**

> Hi [NAME],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. There is a lot of junk science regarding methylated vitamin forms, so we seem to be getting this question often. See below for your answer and be mindful that only B9 (folate) and B12 are available in the market in their methylated forms and the body will convert forms as needed depending on the destination regardless of origin as long as you have enough. The Alln1 SuperBlend (and dotFIT MVMs) uses both B9/Folate (calcium L-5-methyltetrahydrofolate) and B12 (methylcobalamin) in the methylated forms.
>
> Thanks again and have a great day.
>
> Q: Are all your vitamins methylated vitamins? How would I be able to tell?
> A: Your question is vague, in the fact that not all vitamins should contain a methyl donor to be effective (actually it is a problem for some). What you want in any vitamin is an absorbable form (i.e., a form that is already prepared as the body would prepare it once extracted from a food source). All our vitamins (and minerals) are in a pre-formed structure to allow the body proper “functional absorption” (active at the target tissue), which is better than bioavailable absorption. All our labels have the form listed. Do not be fooled that all vitamins need a methyl group to be effective. In all our products, if it needs a methyl group, it will be part of it and listed on the label such as B12 or Folate in our Alln1 SuperBlendTM. If you would like to talk to a chemist further, please reach back out and [NAME] (VP of Product Development) will connect you.
>
> From: [EMAIL]
> Sent: Thursday, October 10, 2024 12:57 PM
> To: Experts <[EMAIL]>
> Subject: Contact Us
>
> Hey buddy,
>
> Something is wrong with whatever your trainer is referring to. He is reading (or hearing from an unqualified influencer) something out of context. Both cyanocobalamin and methylcobalamin are forms the body uses to functionally absorb B12 (arrive at target tissues and go to work), which is why both forms are often used in Multivitamin and Mineral Formulas (MVM). The Alln1 SuperBlendTM only uses methylcobalamin to keep it all vegan. Your trainer is confused by stuff floating around the internet about “methylation”. If he has a series of peer review groundbreaking studies supporting what he thinks he is saying that uses human subjects consuming normal physiologic dosages of B12, please send them with an explanation regarding the methods and materials used to support the conclusion.
>
> In the meantime, he can read some important facts: Cyanocobalamin is a synthetic compound of vitamin B12 used to treat vitamin deficiencies. Chemically, cyanocobalamin is classified as a "corrinoid," representing a crystallizable cobalt complex. The name "cyanocobalamin" is derived from including a cyanide group within the molecule. Cyanocobalamin is approved by the United States Food and Drug Administration (FDA) for pernicious anemia, malabsorption, atrophic gastritis, gastrectomy, Helicobacter pylori infection, and other conditions. Vitamin B12 facilitates several methylation reactions within the body. Methylcobalamin is a cofactor that helps in the conversion of homocysteine to methionine in the body. Furthermore, in the form of adenosylcobalamin, the vitamin is crucial in converting methylmalonyl-coenzyme A (CoA) to succinyl-CoA. Both of these reactions are essential for cell division and growth. Therefore, Vitamin B12 in two forms: methylcobalamin and cyanocobalamin, are important but methylcobalamin is the most effective at being delivered to neurons to support brain health.
>
> See below for more layman confusion, mindful that only B9 (folate) and B12 are available in the market in their methylated forms and the body will generally convert forms as needed depending on final destination regardless of origin as long as you have enough.
>
> Q: Are all your vitamins methylated vitamins? How would I be able to tell?
> A: Your question is vague, in the fact that not all vitamins should contain a methyl donor to be effective (actually it is a problem for some). What you want in any vitamin is an absorbable form (i.e., a form that is already prepared as the body would prepare it once extracted from a food source). All our vitamins (and minerals) are in a pre-formed structure to allow the body proper “functional absorption” (active at the target tissue), which is better than bioavailable absorption. All our labels have the form listed. Do not be fooled that all vitamins need a methyl group to be effective. In all our products, if it needs a methyl group, it will be part of it and listed on the label such as B12 or Folate in our Alln1 SuperBlendTM. If you would like to talk to a chemist further, please reach back out and [NAME] (VP of Product Development) will connect you.

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. The good news is that your member is not an expert, so no one should listen to him on this topic. First of all, many people don’t supplement anything and they are not copper deficient — meaning there is no way the body figures out how to make it automatically 15:2. The body uses what you give it and balances its needs based on levels (the triage factor). The goal is to have all the body can use by supplementing what we know the body doesn’t get from food. That’s what the Alln1 SuperBlendTM and our pill products do perfectly. Refer your member to the PDSRG Vitamin sections (see below). Ironically (and because we want it this way because of normal food intake), the zinc to copper ratio in the Alln1 SuperBlendTM is about 15:2 — 12mg (12000µ) zinc; copper is 800µ (.8gm). Therefore, 12000:800 is ~15:2.
>
> No amount of zinc below the RDA will remove the body’s copper.
>
> Thanks again and have a great weekend.
>
> The daily amounts of vitamins and minerals currently known as the safe and optimal range are captured in the Practitioner's Dietary Supplement Reference Guide (PDSRG) and contained within those documented amounts in the dotFIT formulas — design of the Multivitamin and Mineral Formula (MVM).

> Hey [CUSTOMER],
>
> Nothing more than a California labeling law thing so bottom-feeder attorneys can’t file frivolous lawsuits. It’s been in play for years for products sold in California, but you can’t separate inventories, so it’s easier to put it on most labels since it’s virtually meaningless and is posted in buildings (in fine print) that sell food in California (there is more naturally occurring lead in two leaves of vegetables than any NSF-Certified supplement). Like all our products, MuscleDefender (Glutamine) meets and exceeds all federal and global standards for purity, including parts per million (PPM) of naturally occurring lead content, but California chose to have a warning on all products regardless of meeting federal, global, including NSF, standards.
>
> See the link below. It includes everything you need to know (and more) about Prop 65. Mindful it ONLY applies to California and it’s nothing more than a CYA labeling issue, including posted in all California grocery stores, coffee shops, etc. (unless you are a small company, then you are exempt, which of course makes no sense). There is also an FAQ at the very bottom (once in the link) for a quick answer. Sadly, many companies have had to defend lawsuits for simply not including the Proposition notice on the label, even though the products have met all federal and global standards for naturally originating compounds.
>
> Always good to hear from you, buddy. And enjoy your MuscleDefender, just as the rest of our clients and families do regularly!
>
> https://www.dotfit.com/prop65

> Question: Hello, I am currently taking the All in One and fish oil supplement. I just turned 70. Have been trying Memory Supplements with limited success. In reviewing your brain health supplement. I noticed the large quantities taken compared to other memory supplements. Which causes me to ask the feedback you have received from people taking this. Does it work??
> Answer: The dotFIT BrainHealth supplement (BH) is different than brain/memory supplements that claim to improve memory. The BH supplement is designed to protect the brain from general age-related cognitive decline, which is why the goal is to start using around age 45yrs when normal brain nutrition (see ingredients in BH) is compromised from natural aging including digestion and diet. Further, the reason you see an increase in servings by age is because as we age, we become more resistant to the brain nutrition, thus increasing it according to science, may continue to stave off age-related cognitive decline. In summary, the BH is used to keep your brain intact including preserving memory by feeding it all it can use. Whereas the so-called memory enhancers, which are not evidence-based, claim to bring your memory back (and they generally do not contain brain nutrition).
> At your age, the dosage of the BH can stop at 8-tabs/d rather than going to 12 since we changed the formula to a tableted form (the label still says 3-svg after 65, but it shouldnt be necessary now  but it wont hurt either). The new label (couple of months) will reflect the 1 and 2 serving age amounts, leaving off the 3rd.  I placed some data below from the Practitioner Dietary Supplement Reference Guide (PDSRG) that is used for staff education and certification (not consumer facing) regarding the BH. Most importantly for you would be to make sure your brain is being fed properly, remain active and challenge your brain regularly.
>
> Hope this helps.
>
> From the PDSRG
> Advanced Brain Health1 Formula
> Goal
> To supply nutrition thats often limited by typical diets and the inevitable factors of the natural aging process, and known to support healthy brain function, including potentially reducing the risk of age-related cognitive decline.
> Rationale
> Until late in the 20th century, the basic theory was that we enter adult life with a set number of brain cells that deteriorate gradually until brain function falls apart. During the past 10 years, neuroscientists have proved that this does not need to be the case. The brain does continue to form new connections and to make changes in response to new demands. Like most every other part of the body, the brain is a "use it or lose it" organ. This fact not only means performing regular brain activities but also includes physical activity because exercise helps the brain maintain structural integrity through increased blood flow. Despite this new knowledge, it is common for people to experience overall decline in brain function with age. This tendency may be influenced by reduced use of the brain, sedentary lifestyles2 and by common limitations in brain nutrition.
> Several substances are showing the potential to support brain function and to slow (and possibly stop or reverse) age-related decline in mental function. Among these substances, phosphatidylserine (PS), acetyl-L-carnitine (ALC), alpha-lipoic acid (ALA), and vitamin B-12 have been found to offer support to the maintenance of aging brain function. Following the middle-age years, supplementation with these compounds may balance a decline in the bodys production or absorption of these substances that are essential for normal brain and neurological function.,,, Clinical findings support the benefit of nutritional supplements for cognitive performance and mood/behavior and suggest that additional supplementation may be required for the elderly.,,,

>  Take two (2) scoops 20-30 minutes after post workout AminoFormula dose
>  Use anytime throughout the day as a supplement to a meal or by itself mixed with desired ingredients to add protein/calories as needed to meet daily requirements.
> Be sure to ingest approximately one (1) g of protein per pound of lean body mass (or weight if not overweight) daily from all sources including foods divided 4-5 times daily along with the pre/post shakes (the pre/post shakes combined generally supply 50-80 g of the daily requirement)
>
> AminoFormula (AF) (workout days only)
>  Take one scoop approximately 10 minutes before workout (add A0to NO7Rage if using this product 145; see below). You may continue to drink during workout.
>  Take one (1) scoop immediately following workout.
>  Or use both scoops and start ingesting 10min before workout and consume at a pace so it79s finished at end of activity
>

> 2nd Week Start CreatineMonohydrate (CrM)*
>  Loading phase: mix one (1) scoop (5 g) with 4-8 oz of favorite fluid/shake and take four (4) times daily with a protein and/or carbohydrate containing meal/drink for first five (5) days.
> On training days, use one dose before workout and one after with meals/drinks. May mix with your pre/post training formula.
>  Maintenance phase: after five (5) day loading phase:
> On training days Take one (1) scoop daily with post workout shake.
> On non-training days take 1scoop with any meal when not taking ECX (i.e., use ECX at different times so total creatine is evenly dispersed throughout the day)
> Persons <175LBS can discontinue CrM dosing when starting ECX, b/c ECX daily dosing will maintain CrM stores
>
> Add ExtremeCreatineXXXL (ECX) on 6th day of 2nd Week
> Two (2) scoops supply 3.2 g of beta-alanine (BA), 5 g of creatine monohydrate and 7 g of L-glutamine
>   Take 2-scoops daily. Take with some protein and/or carbs but within allotted calories based on body composition goal.
> Training days: take one (1) scoop before training with pre-workout full meal (generally 2-3 hours pre-workout). If not possible based on early training, take with pre-workout shake. Take remaining dose (1 scoop) any time with meal or shake when not taking a CreatineMonohydrate dose to help evenly spread total creatine intake throughout the day
> Non-training days: 1-scoop with morning meal or shake and 1-scoop with evening meal or shake
>
> Add NO7Rage at 4th week (workout days only)
>  Take 1.5-2.5 scoops depending on body weight (caffeine sensitivity- start with 1.5 and increase to recommended dose if not uncomfortably affected by caffeine) approximately 10 minutes before workout (may mix with AF and continue to consume during workout). See directions on label for weight dosages.
> NO7Rage contains 150mg of caffeine per scoop. As a reference: Starbucks Grande-drip coffee contains ~330 mg of caffeine, close to the same amount in two (2) scoops of NO7Rage.
>
> Total daily creatine for this plan: Loading phase 20 g/d. Thereafter until fourth week 10g/d (or 5g if <175LBS). At fourth week on workout days 12.5-15g/day and 10g on non-workout days (half this if <175LBS). Total BA intake 6.2- 8.2 g/day on workout days 3.2 g on non-workout days. To maximize uptake, creatine intake should be spread as evenly as possible throughout the day and around the workout as described and ingested with some carbohydrates and/or protein.

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. Yes, calcium carbonate is considered the better form of calcium partially because of cost and bioavailability with food. See below for more information from the Practitioner Dietary Supplement Reference Guide (PDSRG). Also be mindful that the SuperCalcium contains the necessary cofactors (e.g., Vitamin D, magnesium, and both forms of vitamin K) to properly deposit the mineral in bone. And it’s NSF-Certified for Sport.
>
> Thanks again and have a great day.
>
> More Info
>
> The two common forms of calcium supplements are calcium citrate and carbonate, with properly prepared calcium carbonate showing superior absorption when ingested with food, which is when we recommend taking a calcium supplement — i.e., take with meals. The carbonate form also has the highest content of calcium by weight, allowing less expense and a smaller pill size containing more calcium than citrate and other forms.38F Calcium carbonate is 40% calcium by weight while calcium citrate is only 21%.39 Further, calcium citrate may inhibit absorption of non-heme iron while other forms, including carbonate, show little to no effect. However, calcium citrate may have a clinical application since it does not require food-stimulated acids for digestion.

**Source answer:**

> Hi [NAME],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. Yes, you can use the fat loss supplements in your Lean Pack 90 simultaneously, and it is commonly done when people are in a hurry to lose fat (behind schedule) or need extra help. The Lean Pak90 is designed to last 90 days by using each product for 30 days and following the diet information, but many people do use them simultaneously. Simply use each one as directed daily.
>
> FAQs
> Q: Can I take all Lean Pack 90 products daily or individually over the 90 days?
> A: You can use the products as they are designed for the kit, meaning using the Weight Loss and Liver Support the first 30 days; CarbRepel the second 30 days; and end with ThermAccel for the last 30 days for a total of 90 days. Or you can use them simultaneously if you need extra assistance or have a tight timeline to lose weight since they all work by different mechanisms.
>
> Q: Do you recommend CarbRepel (CR) or Weight Loss & Liver Support (WLLS) and ThermAccel (TA) used together if I want to boost weight/fat loss?
> A: Yes, you can use CarbRepel with ThermAccel, or any of the dotFIT weight loss products such as Weight Loss and Liver Support together, and it is commonly done with people needing extra help, as each product works to support appetite and metabolism through different and unique mechanisms. Simply follow the directions on each product label. The links will take you to a brief product description including a short video.
>
> Q: Should I still take CarbRepel before a meal if I am not eating carbs at that meal? What if I forget to take it 30 minutes before a meal?
> A: No, you would not use CarbRepel if not consuming carbs, or very little carbs such as only vegetables or a little fruit. Taking CarbRepel as it was used in the clinical trials is ideal (~30 minutes before a carb-containing meal), but it will still work if you take it at/before, or very close to, the meal.
>
> Q: Should I still take Weight Loss & Liver Support in the morning if I'm not going to eat breakfast? What if I forget to take it 30 minutes before a meal?
> A: The bottom line is that Weight Loss & Liver Support should be consumed three times daily and separated as evenly as possible to help maintain the desired levels of ingredients. Taking it before a meal is one way of spreading it out and getting help in appetite support within 30 minutes. But it’s not necessary once you start using the product regularly. The goal is to keep it consistent so it works all day, because each dose brings the levels back up to maximum effectiveness. And yes, have your client take it in the morning even if not consuming breakfast.
>
> Thanks again and have a great day.

>   o Non-training days, take 1 scoop with morning meal or shake and one (1) scoop with evening meal or shake.

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. We are not sure what you are asking here, other than there is no evidence that chromium supplementation at a proper daily amount* is toxic anywhere in the body. If you are referring to higher doses (we can’t tell a dose you are referring to from what you wrote), the answer is that any metal/mineral can be toxic at high levels - i.e., daily ingestion is regularly above the Tolerable Upper Limit Levels (UL) and chromium would be no exception. That said, chromium up to 1000 mcg daily is likely safe for short-term use, and doses of 200-1000 mcg daily have been used safely for up to 2 years. And there is no established Tolerable Upper Limit (UL) for chromium at this time.
>
> Hope this helps – let us know if you have any questions.
>
> Have a great weekend.
>
> *Chromium
> Adequate Intake (AI): The AI is an estimated amount of chromium that is generally considered sufficient for most people.
> Men (19-50 years): 35 mcg
> Women (19-50 years): 25 mcg
> Older adults (51+ years): 30 mcg for men and 20 mcg for women

- (item-specific) …

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. We don’t think much of L-carnitine as a dietary supplement for fitness goals including performance or body fat reduction in healthy athletes/exercisers. L-carnitine is used clinically with success in cardiovascular-compromised humans (e.g., deficiency states, some cardiovascular/metabolic settings, and nerve/neuropathy contexts). If your goals are fat loss or acute performance, carnitine is not a primary driver. Proper energy balance, protein, creatine, beta-alanine, caffeine (i.e., evidence-based dietary supplements), progressive overload, and sleep will wash out any benefit of carnitine supplementation. Carnitine can be a nice-to-have for recovery at high dosages (2–4 g/day) and may nudge weight metrics downward over months—especially in higher-BMI clients—as long as the rest of the plan is tight.
>
> Hope this helps.
>
> Thanks again and have a great Sunday night.

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. Fueling bodybuilding workouts adequately with carbohydrates (CHO) is unique to your training phase, i.e., off-season (bulking/pure muscle gain focus) or in season (preparing for competition). Moreover, hypertrophy and strength training do not require the same amount of CHO/LB to optimize a training session when compared to endurance training. In a nutshell, after training, for off-season bodybuilders (BB), make sure your CHO intake is ~2:1 CHO/Protein as it should be most of the day. In season, obviously, begin to slowly reduce CHO intake all the way around, but still have most allowed CHO before and after the workout. I attached a PDF of a lecture I do for our licensed facilities and certified practitioners that summarizes everything you need to know about optimizing body composition based on the goal (e.g., losing bodyfat while gaining muscle; gaining muscle only; contest prep).
>
> Thanks again, have a great day and good luck in your bodybuilding journey!
>
> PS
> Hopefully your server has enough space to accept the attachment. Let us know if not.

That said, your blood thinning medications (warfarin) should not interfere with a baseline dietary support program (including the correct dose of vitamin K) unless your doctor knows something we do not about your unique physiology – so make sure you inform your doctor of anything you decide to incorporate into your daily routine.
>
> Creatine is also a great addition to your baseline program. There should be no need for NAD or NMD when you are following a complete metabolism reboot program such as below. You can skip the creatine loading phase and just start with the maintenance, which is 5-10gm/d based on weight. ≥175LBS use 10gm/d; < 175 use 5gm.
>
> Thanks again and have a great evening, ([NAME] is cc’d here in case you need more information.)
>
> General Sport Health, Activity Recovery, Performance Program - CM In Baseline
> Click on product links for a brief product description including short video
>
> Daily:
> - dotFIT Multivitamin & Mineral for your age and gender. If 50yrs or older use Over50 unless noted below; if female under 50 use Women’s; Males and very active females (competitive training protocols) under 50 use 2-Active, with both genders continuing use till 65yrs if still training competitively at which time every person will use Over50; children under 12yr use Kids; children 12-17yr use 1-Active.
>
> Take as directed with meals
>
> CreatineMonohydrate Loading & Maintenance Strategy (if you are under 175LBS you can cut the daily maintenance dose in half*  - i.e., after loading period, take a total of 5gms daily and split as described below)
> Mix 1 scoop (5gms) with 4-8oz of favorite fluid/shake and take 4 times daily with a protein and/or carbohydrate containing meal/drink for first 5 days. Thereafter take 1-scoop twice daily to maintain stores. To help maximize creatine uptake, split doses throughout the day with meals/drinks containing protein and/or carbohydrate.
>
> Training days: take 1-dose before workout & 1 after with meals/drinks. May mix with your pre & post training formula.
>
> Non-training days* take 1-dose with AM meal and 1-dose with PM meal.
>
> *Programs total daily creatine first 5 days: 20gms/d. Maintenance phase: all days 5-10gms. To maximize uptake, creatine intake should be spread as evenly as possible throughout the day with some protein and/or carb containing meals or shakes.
>
> Daily as needed:
> - WheySmooth or other favorite dotFIT protein mix (based on caloric allotment for goal)
>   Use as directed to make sure you get ~1gm of protein per pound of LBM (or weight if not overweight) daily from all sources including foods divided 3-5 times daily
>   Can be used as a supplement to a meal or by itself (or mixed with desired supporting contents) to shore up total daily protein needs
> - Super Calcium (read all food labels for calcium content)
>   Use if you do not meet the daily needs of calcium (~1000-1200 mgs) from food/shake intake
>   Females take 1 or 2 daily with meals; if you need to take 2, take one with AM meal and the 2nd with PM meal; Males take 1 only if necessary
> - Super Omega 3
>   Take 1-2 daily with meal if not consuming 8-16oz/wk fatty fish (take 1 if >8oz/wk but <16; 2 if ≤8oz); not necessary if consuming ≥16oz/wk unless for athletic recovery purposes, then increase as directed
>
> Workout days
> - Creatine Monohydrate
>   On training days, use one dose before workout and one after with meals/drinks. May mix with your pre & post training formula.

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us and thanks for the thoughtful questions. The amount of vitamin E in any supplement should be based on the population you serve, which is what ours does. I’m guessing you are a certified Practitioner, therefore, you realize you have all the answers to your questions related to evidence-based dosages for all dotFIT products in the Practitioner Dietary Supplement Reference Guide (PDSRG). If not, I have cc’d [NAME] here to make sure you know how to get access to your science supporting proper recommendations.
>
> Quick answer is that the Vitamin E in our Active and other dotFIT multivitamin and mineral formula (MVM) including the Alln1 SuperBlend™ is in the proper daily amounts to support active individuals and does not exceed the Tolerable Upper Limit Levels (UL). The Food and Nutrition Board of the Institute of Medicine has set the UL for vitamin E at 1,000 mg (1,500 IU) per day for supplemental vitamin E. Further, you mention that few persons are Vitamin E deficient (agreed—see true definition of a deficiency, which is death if not corrected). However, most persons are vitamin E insufficient. Meaning nothing works optimally where Vitamin E is involved, which is about 300 different necessary life functions. 86% of the US population is below the RDA and almost that much below the EAR. Therefore, vitamin E is listed as a nutrient of concern in the Dietary Guidelines for Americans. All this makes it an important supplement for our population being active individuals (there will always be sub-populations that should not have certain amounts of specific nutrients – but that is a clinical issue, and they should know this from their health history). We are on the protective and daily recovery side with the appropriate amounts of supplemental VMs to correct the insufficiencies of western diets for our population.
>
> Below is more information including a friendly reminder of the population we work with, which is satisfying nutrition requirements of humans that want to remain healthy and active. We own this domain along with sports performance/fitness performance.
>
> Thanks again and have a great weekend.
>
> More Info.
> The vitamin and mineral amounts in all the dotFIT formulas are best in science today (unless you have an unusual contraindicated medical condition) since it was designed by vitamin and mineral researchers to complement the western diets in industrialized nations. In other words, not just to fill gaps but to also offer protection against the common maladies of aging. All due respect to the studies you may cite, they simply call out an association that does not apply to the general active population.
>
> The bottom line is that the nutrient RDAs in the DRIs including Daily Values, are set/designed to correct deficiencies, and not meant to be optimal. Moreover, the safe and optimal range is well above the RDAs for many vitamins (e.g., C, D, E, B-vitamins, etc.) and some minerals, but never over the Tolerable Upper Intake Levels (UL), which is the end range (see chart in your PDSRG).
>
> The daily amounts of vitamins and minerals currently known as the safe and optimal range, are captured the peer review referenced Practitioner's Dietary Supplement Reference Guide (PDSRG) and contained within those documented amounts in the dotFIT formulas - - design of the Multivitamin and Mineral Formula (MVM).
>
> More information on vitamin and minerals daily intakes above the RDAs
> Current evidence-based supplemental amounts of vitamins and minerals for active individuals that support long and short-term health are contained in the dotFIT formulas including the Alln1 SuperBlendTM, especially the primary antioxidant vitamins C and E. Further, none of the ingredients are at or over the known ULs (they are all in the known established safe and optimal range). The complete rationale for each ingredient is from the world’s leading experts and found in the Practitioner's Dietary Supplement Reference Guide (PDSRG) that is used for certification and practitioner education (not consumer facing). All references are from peer review published research and the formulas are 3rd party tested including most having an additional NSF Certified for sport (meaning it also meets all worldwide safety, label claims and drug testing regulations). The product is formulated by the experts at dotFIT. Feel free to send the information links to your customer or dietician. The content will be a good education for the inquisitive person. Have your contact click on the appropriate links that explain the design of the Multivitamin and Mineral Formula (MVM) and then they can click on links to the peer-review referenced article that sets the foundation found in the PDSRG document.

**Source answer:**

> Bloating is a funny thing and it’s almost unavoidable in perimenopause and menopause no matter what you eat. So, the first question I ask females is age. Then all you can do is play around with different foods to minimize the effects.
>
> As far as detox goes, on its face (detoxing impurities in the body), it’s pure quackery. But everyone has different definitions of detoxing/cleansing. For instance, the product she names contains milk thistle and choline. Both ingredients are in the Weight Loss and Liver Support (WLLS) product that work to help metabolize fat droplets in the liver and subsequently support healing liver tissues and improving energy metabolism. The problem with the product she names, JSHEALTH Detox + Debloat Formula, is that both those ingredients are well under their respective effective dosages. The effective doses are in the WLLS at a daily dose of 400 mg of choline and 498 mg of milk thistle, whereas they only have 82 mg of choline and 120 mg of milk thistle in a full daily dose. Further, their turmeric amount is useless. Bottom line is if someone is using this JS Health product to somehow cleanse/detox their body, it is useless. If they are using it to minimize bloating, and it somehow works—well, ok. Not sure how, but anything is possible depending on the physiological state of the recipient.
>
> Below is a related FAQ from the website.
>
> All articles https://www.dotfit.com/search.aspx?q=detox
>
> What is the best way to detoxify my body? https://www.dotfit.com/content-35775.html
>
> You don't need to. The notion that fasting cleanses actually “clean” the body is more of a marketing ploy than sound science. The human body naturally expels toxins via detoxification pathways in the gallbladder, liver, kidneys, digestive system, lungs and skin. The gastrointestinal tract contains 70% of the body’s immune response and one of its major roles is to remove waste from the body. Currently, there is little to no research that waste build-up or constipation (straining to pass hard stools) causes cancer, health issues or disease. Most importantly, follow your Playspan® dietary support program to calorically efficiently meet your protein and vitamin and mineral recommendations. That said, if still compelled to clean up your lifestyle for some period of time, adding any of the following strategies to your lifestyle is easy, healthy and your best bet at "detoxifying" the body: https://www.dotfit.com/content-35775.html.
>
> 1. Minimize added sugars such as soda and sugar-sweetened beverages
> 2. Eat a diet rich in fresh organic fruits and vegetables. These foods contain fiber, protective phytonutrients and vitamins and minerals
> 3. Consume plant oils and unprocessed nuts for anti-inflammatory oils and minerals.
> 4. Eat adequate protein from high quality sources, fresh eggs, and vegetables. If you eat meat, choose sources that are humanely treated, free of hormones and antibiotics
> 5. For individuals with allergies, choose foods and supplements that are free of common food allergens such as gluten (from grains) and casein (from dairy)
> 6. Eliminate stimulants, caffeine, alcohol, tobacco products, modified food ingredients and chemicals and drink adequate amounts of clean water free of contaminants. For pure water consider a reverse osmosis filter system or RO water which can be found at many natural grocery and health food stores
> 7. Consume adequate fiber - 28 to 35 grams a day to promote proper bowel function
> 8. Get enough restorative sleep (7.5 to 9 hrs/night of sound sleep or stage 4 sleep is optimal)
> 9. Make an effort to intake specific nutrients that have been found to support proper detoxification function, including epigallocatechin gallate from green tea, glucosinolates from cruciferous vegetables (broccoli, cauliflower, Brussel sprouts, and cabbage), resveratrol from grapes and peanut skins, isoflavones from soybeans or fermented soy, and polyphenols and anthocyanidins from berries
> 10. Try hot lemon water with cayenne pepper before breakfast to stimulate the liver.
> 11. Add a tablespoon of apple cider vinegar in fresh water for the kidneys
> 12. Exercise enough to sweat to help open the pores to release toxins through the skin
> 13. Try body brushing and rebounding to stimulate lymph function for the immune system

> Females take 1 or 2 daily with meals; if you need to take 2, take one with AM meal and the 2nd with PM meal; Males take 1 only if necessary
>
> Super Omega 3
> Take 1-2 daily with meal if not consuming 8-16oz/wk fatty fish (take 1 if >8oz/wk but <16; 2 if ≤8oz); not necessary if consuming ≥16oz/wk unless for athletic recovery purposes, then increase as directed

> Vitamin D (to achieve serum 25(OH)D concentration of 40-50 ng/ml)
> Take 2 daily till next blood test
> Super Calcium (read all food labels for calcium content)
> Use if you do not meet the daily needs of calcium (~1200 mgs) from food/shake intake
> If needed take 1 with a meal
> SuperOmega-3
> Take 2 daily with any meal if not consuming 4svgs fatty fish weekly
> CreatineMonohydrate (CrM)
> Take 1-scoop (5 gm) daily anytime with a meal or in your AminoFormula shake
> AminoFormula (AF) count as 25-40 gms to daily protein
> Take 1-scoop twice daily between meals to shore up protein needs
> Use if necessary to complete a low protein meal to help meet daily protein requirement of ~1 gram of protein per pound of LBM
>
> Optional
> Favorite dotFIT protein mix
> Use as directed to make sure you get ~1 gm of protein per pound of LBM (or weight if not overweight) daily from all sources including foods divided 3-5 times daily
> Can be used as a supplement to a meal or by itself (or mixed with desired supporting contents) to shore up total daily protein needs

**Source answer:**

> FAQ
> Q: Can you explain when I would need Creatine Monohydrate vs extreme creatine XXXL?
> A: Using ExtremeCreatineXXXL+ (EC) versus CreatineMonohydrate (CM) is simply based on your goals. If your fitness goal is to improve performance beyond just taking CM (along with your baseline dietary support, e.g., multi, omega and protein as needed), then you would use EC because it contains the same CM but also contains beta-alanine, Careflow (vasodilator) for added muscle endurance and force production along with glutamine for added exercise recovery.
>
> See below for a related FAQ.
>
> HI [NAME],
> Thanks for contacting us. See below for your answer along with some other info and related FAQ.
> Thanks again and have a great Monday
>
> FAQ
> Q: What is the difference between the dotFIT ExtremeCreatineXXXL and CreatineMonohydrate?
> A: CreatineMonohydrate is simply the creatine monohydrate. ExtremeCreatineXXXL is the same creatine monohydrate (NSF-CS) but also contains beta-alanine, glutamine and Carflow™ (vasodilator) for their respective additive performance benefits. For a product description including short video go to the dotFIT website store and click on the product you would like to learn about. Here are the direct links to the 2 products you requested CreatineMonohydrate and ExtremeCreatineXXXXL+. Once you click in then go to “Overview” to get product details and video.
>
> More Info
>
> Dosing ExtremeCreatineXXXL+ as a Standalone Product
> ExtremeCreatineXXXL+ to maintain all till end of cycle
> On training days, take one (1) scoop before training and one (1) scoop after. You may use along with your pre, and post workout shake or meal containing protein and/or carbohydrate.
> For those users >150LBS and seeking to maximize performance such as strength, speed and power activities, add a third scoop any other time of the day with a meal or shake
> On non-training days (to maintain Cr, BA & CF) levels, take one (1) scoop with morning meal or shake and one (1) scoop with evening meal or shake. If using three (3) scoops, spread each scoop throughout the day with meals/shake – i.e., morning, afternoon and early evening. (true Creatine only supplementation maintenance is .045g/lb/d).
>
> Dosing ExtremeCreatineXXXL+ within Size and Performance Stacking Protocols See complete supplement stacking programs for competitive size and performance athletes here: Creatine Muscle & Performance Stacks
>
> CreatineMonohydrate as a standalone product
> Creatine Loading & Maintenance Strategy* (Refer to Mix Dosing & Stacking Instructions in Appendix)
> CreatineMonohydrate Loading (true loading .14g/lb/d) & Maintenance* Strategy (true maintenance .045g/lb/d)
> Mix 1 scoop (5gms) with 4-8oz of favorite fluid/shake and take 4 times daily with a protein and/or carbohydrate containing meal/drink for first 5 days. Thereafter take 1-scoop twice daily to maintain stores. To help maximize creatine uptake, split doses throughout the day with meals/drinks containing protein and/or carbohydrate
> Training days: 1 dose before workout & 1 after with meals/drinks. May mix with your pre & post training formula
> Non-training days take 1-scoop with AM meal and 1-scoop with PM meal
> *If under 175LBS you can cut the daily maintenance dose in half - i.e., after loading period, take a total of 5gms daily and split as described or all in 1 dose daily

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
> Thanks for contacting us. In a nutshell, both ExtremeCreatineXXXL+(EC) and NO7Rage used properly, would most likely contribute to muscle size and growth equally since they contain like ingredients other than the caffeine in NO7, which has unique performance enhancing benefits. EC is a non-stimulant multi-ingredient pre-workout product while NO7 has the stimulant effect (caffeine) along with taurine and glycerol. Both products contain creatine, beta-alanine and vasodilators (EC uses Careflow, while NO7 uses citrulline malate and Nitrosigine). **Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us and sorry for the delayed response. All our products are 3rd party tested after completion, and most also go through the NSF Certified for Sport (NSF-CS) process (a "4th party" test, if you will), which is a significant extra cost per lot and goes beyond the standard NSF certification and 3rd party testing. Therefore, products that are not in high demand, such as some fat loss products including LeanMR or Weight Loss & Liver Support (WLLS), are not routinely sent through the additional NSF-CS process so we can keep these products affordable for everyone; if an organization requires NSF-CS (for example pro and college teams or other drug-tested athlete organizations), we will arrange NSF-CS testing for those lots. Again, all products are 3rd party tested by outside labs (for example Natural Products Association’s GMP certification, Covance, LA Analytical & Chemical Solutions), are manufactured in NSF-certified facilities, and go through the same manufacturing rigor and processes, so the only reason for the extra NSF-CS (a "4th party" test) is for customers or organizations that require it.
>
> Thanks again and have a great day.

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. Both iron and zinc are contained in all Practitioner complete multivitamin and mineral formulas (MVM) (exceptions are for some people over 50 years where extra iron can be a problem) because they are 2 of the 20 known potentially under-consumed nutrients in US diets and because our Practitioner MVMs are in a controlled release matrix, all ingredients make it to their target tissues. Further, many of our Practitioners read their clients' biomarkers before and after supplementation and find that all iron- and zinc-dependent enzymes/proteins are improved, validating efficacy. With all due respect, you have probably misinterpreted the studies or simply taken them out of context. There would be a lot of dying humans if they could not absorb all essential nutrition regardless of how they are consumed since that is the way humans consume foods/mixed meals. And both zinc and iron are essential to life. The human body developed an amazing digestive tract that can present the nutrients from food and supplements in the proper forms to be absorbed into the body to maintain life.
>
> If you would like to learn more about designing the best possible MVM formula that complement the western diets I have included a section (links below) from the Practitioner Dietary Supplement Reference Guide (PDSRG) that our Practitioners, including pharmacists and dieticians, use for training and education.
>
> Thanks again and have a great day.
>
> The daily amounts of vitamins and minerals currently known as the safe and optimal range are captured in the peer review referenced Practitioner's Dietary Supplement Reference Guide (PDSRG) and contained within those documented amounts in the dotFIT formulas — design of the Multivitamin and Mineral Formula (MVM).

## G-207 [dev] 2026 — (untagged)

**Q:** Have [NAME] and [NAME] ever done a podcast on reverse dieting, and how can I determine reliable resources about reverse dieting?

*As originally asked:* Hello! I was wondering if Neal and Zane have ever done a podcast on reverse dieting? I trust their knowledge and advice, and I keep hearing conflicting arguments and I’m not sure which resources to trust, or how to research this correctly on my own. THANK YOU for sharing your knowledge!

*thread_date: 2026-05-15 · topics: dieting strategies, metabolism, refeeding, reverse dieting, weight maintenance · source: `9ab5b7bbdcaeaaa8` 2026/Reverse dieting.docx*

**Points to hit (2–5):**

- 1. …
- 2. …
- 3. …
- 4. …
- 5. …

**Expected sources:**

- [ ] QA `9ab5b7bbdcaeaaa8` — 2026/Reverse dieting.docx (authority 3)
- [ ] podcast segment (authority 4)
- [ ] other: …

**Forbidden:**

- disease-treatment/cure/prevention language beyond products.json wording (claims are quoted, never paraphrased); medication or managed-condition guidance (escalate per §8); dosage arithmetic the source answer does not do.
- (item-specific) …

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us and digging for the facts through the SuppBeast.
>
> Honestly, a lot of the confusion around 'reverse dieting' comes from the name itself because it makes it sound like some new or special fat-loss strategy when it's really not. Reverse dieting originally came from the bodybuilding world (me included) after extremely restrictive contest diets, where calories were brought back up slowly to avoid rapid rebound fat gain while returning to a more sustainable intake. For example, if a competitor had dieted down to 1,500 calories for a show, they might gradually increase by 100–150 calories per week until they found their maintenance level without suddenly overeating (and, of course, our exercise output was changing dramatically after the competition as well — so that was factoring into the calorie intake as well). That's all reverse dieting really is — a controlled return to maintenance calories after aggressive dieting.
>
> Today, social media often presents reverse dieting as some sort of metabolism-hacking magic method, but the science really doesn't support that idea. It doesn't suddenly 'repair' metabolism or allow people to eat unlimited calories without gaining body fat. What it can do is help someone transition out of a restrictive diet in a more structured and psychologically controlled way, and, as the calories go up, the person may want to be more active, helping them burn more calories. So, for most healthy exercisers and gym members, the focus should be less on chasing trendy terms like 'reverse dieting' and more on building sustainable eating habits, adequate protein intake, resistance training, and maintaining a calorie level that supports the immediate goal and long-term results without extremes.
>
> So your quick takeaway is that 'Reverse dieting' isn't a magic metabolism trick — it's simply a gradual return to maintenance calories after aggressive dieting to help minimize rebound weight gain and regain a sustainable eating pattern. The real long-term success still comes from consistent nutrition habits, adequate protein, resistance training, and avoiding extreme dieting in the first place.
>
> Hope this helps and thanks for listening to facts as they unfold on all things nutrition and weight control on the SuppBeast.
>
> Have a great weekend.

- (item-specific) …

**Source answer:**

> The aminos are all from microbial fermentation.
>
> Fermented Amino Acid Benefits
>
> Reduced Pollution: The fermentation process primarily utilizes microbial metabolic activities, leading to significantly lower waste water, waste gas, and waste residue generation compared to chemical synthesis methods.
>
> Resource Utilization of Waste: The residue from fermentation contains microbial biomass and unused nutrients, which can be converted into organic fertilizers, achieving a circular use of waste.
>
> Mild Process: Compared to chemical methods, which are energy-intensive and highly polluting, fermentation takes place under mild conditions, at low temperatures, and with minimal environmental impact.
>
> High Purity: The main metabolic product of fermentation is amino acids, which have a low impurity content and high purity, meeting safety standards required in the food and pharmaceutical industries.
>
> No Pollution Risks: The fermentation process is conducted under strict sterile conditions, effectively preventing the introduction of harmful substances.
>
> Avoidance of Harmful Residues: Fermentation avoids the potential heavy metal ions or organic solvent residues that may occur during chemical synthesis, making it more suitable for applications that require high safety standards.
>
> Inexpensive Raw Materials: The primary raw materials used in fermentation are carbon and nitrogen sources, which are abundant and have stable prices.
>
> Diverse Production: Fermentation can produce a wide range of amino acids, and customized production can be achieved by optimizing fermentation conditions.
>
> Technological Scalability: With the advancement of biotechnology, there is still room for improvement in fermentation efficiency and cost reduction, offering vast prospects for future industrial development.
>

## G-210 [test] 2026 — (untagged)

**Q:** If two 25 lb BULK whey bags (chocolate and vanilla) weigh the same, why do their published serving counts differ?

*As originally asked:* UFC is inquiring about the difference in serving sizes for the BULK. They asked if they both weigh the same, why are the servings different between the chocolate and vanilla?

*thread_date: — · topics: bulk-weight, flavor-difference, manufacturing-tolerance, serving-size · source: `f671e5e13f0f4287` 2026/Bulk Whey overages expalnation, diff in vanilla & chocolate.docx*

**Expected sources:**

- [ ] QA `f671e5e13f0f4287` — 2026/Bulk Whey overages expalnation, diff in vanilla & chocolate.docx (authority 3)
- [ ] podcast segment (authority 4)
- [ ] other: …

**Forbidden:**

- disease-treatment/cure/prevention language beyond products.json wording (claims are quoted, never paraphrased); medication or managed-condition guidance (escalate per §8); dosage arithmetic the source answer does not do.
- (item-specific) …

**Source answer:**

> The serving counts differ because chocolate and vanilla have slightly different serving weights, and the manufacturer rounds fill weights to ensure customers receive at least the labeled amount. Chocolate has a slightly larger serving size (42 g vs. 41 g for vanilla), so the published serving counts are estimates based on flavor-specific serving sizes and manufacturing fill tolerances. Because the manufacturer rounds fill weights to cover the labeled amounts, serving counts can differ between flavors — in practice customers often receive slightly more product than the labeled minimum. To illustrate with the batch numbers provided: 25 lb equals 11,339.8 g. 284 servings of chocolate at 42 g/serving = 11,928 g (~26.29 lb). 302 servings of vanilla at 41 g/serving = 12,382 g (~27.2 lb). Going off a pure 25 lb (no rounding): 11,339.8 ÷ 42 g = 269 servings (chocolate); 11,339.8 ÷ 41 g = 276 servings (vanilla). The rounding up on bulk fill favors the customer, which is why the label says “about” a certain number of servings — you are getting more than the minimum in both scenarios. Ask [NAME] if it is ok to say all this. From the manufacturer: Yes, we are aligned with [NAME]’s answer.

>
> To understand how our program works to estimate EE please see below:
> More info
> Although the most accurate measurement for free living energy expenditure (EE), the Doubly Labeled Water (DLW) technique is very expensive, relatively invasive and definitely not practical for monitoring EE. Basically it’s used for scientific study. Therefore, we designed the dotFIT program to work without having to perform this type of monitoring. To accomplish this, we base all starting and ongoing calorie recommendations on the latest, most accurate formulations combined with your true body/weight measurement changes and trends, leaving no room for error on the program calorie recommendations that will keep you on track to your goal.
>
> Energy Expenditure Formulation
> The purpose of estimating your energy expenditure is to give you the menus containing calorie totals that will get you started toward your stated goal. From that point on, your changing measurements will dictate your future, accurate calorie intake program recommendations.
>
> The dotFIT Program estimates your AVERAGE daily energy expenditure (EE) based on the following:
> Your basal metabolic rate (BMR) using your physical characteristics and the Mifflin St. Jeor formula (MJF)
> Your sleeping metabolic rate based on the hours you say you generally sleep and the MJF
> Your average daily activities based on your selection (1-7 below) from the program questionnaire that best describes your AVERAGE week:
> I spend most of the day sitting and I do not exercise.
> I spend most of the day sitting and I exercise less than 4 times a week.
> I spend most of the day sitting and I exercise at least 5 times a week, OR I perform continuous light activity but do not exercise.
> I spend most of the day sitting and I exercise at least 5 times a week, OR I perform continuous light activity and exercise less than 3 times a week.
> I perform light continuous activity and I exercise at least 3 times a week, OR I perform continuous moderate activity and exercise less than 3 times a week.
> I train multiple times per day, OR I perform moderate continuous activity and exercise at least 3 times a week.
> I am an endurance athlete training competitively, OR I perform heavy continuous activity and exercise at least 3 times a week.
>
> Incorporating all the above logic allows an extremely accurate view of how many daily calories you burn on average. Even if you burn more on one or two days of the week by adding more work/exercise, research has demonstrated that you generally will unknowingly compensate by burning less during the rest of the same day or on other days (due to decreased overall daily movement) thus maintaining the accuracy of your estimated weekly or monthly calorie burn. For this reason, we do not allow users to add what they might think of as additional DAILY burned calories to an AVERAGE daily burn as it would give them the sense of a larger energy deficit than actual.* And keep in mind, the only way to validate that you are not compensating for sporadic increases in daily activities would be to use an accurate calorie expenditure measuring method such as DLW for at least two weeks, and preferably a month.
>
> *We do however, allow you to enter your exercise into the program and show it as “calories burned from exercise” but it’s not added to your average daily EE because it’s already in there based on the way you answered the 7 multipliers and the above explanation.
>
> Regularly burning more calories than estimated or losing weight quicker than desired
> If a long-term change in your daily routine occurs, you may need to update your answers to the program questionnaire regarding the seven options above. Just choose your “current activity” descriptor accordingly to support either your increase in average daily calorie burn that forces you to continually perform more exercise while still maintaining the same daily activities (e.g. longer cardio sessions, more days per week, etc.), or vice-versa, you increase daily activities without decreasing exercise (e.g. job change, hobby, etc.).
>
> Your body measurement/weight change is the answer to your true calorie balance, not your inputs or estimates
> The program’s calorie recommendations and ongoing calorie adjustments are always accurate because they are tied to your goal and weekly or bi-weekly measurement changes – not your calorie burn or the calories you reported consuming. These numbers are nice to know; they give you something to add and subtract from, and help hold you accountable but do not have to be true. Measurement changes create an indisputable trend and ongoing calorie deficit recommendations that are tied to these changes and the mathematical laws of energy. Follow the program feedback and you will reach your body composition goal. In other words, when the feedback tells you to increase your deficit to XXcals/day – do it, regardless of what you believe you previously did.
>
> Note: If you are interested in how many calories certain activities/exercises burn, use our calorie burn tool, record them wherever you like (they will also appear on your Summary Page). If you perform this work and attempting weight loss, think of it only as a bonus towards your weight loss goal, not necessarily a license to eat more.
>
> From a previous FAQ:
> How dotFIT Me™ Sets Your Goal & Keeps You on Track
> Estimating Energy Expenditure Creates Your Initial Calorie Recommendations
> Your estimated average daily energy expenditure (expected calories burned) is a calculation taken from your personal statistics and a complete week’s view of your typical daily activities. This daily average of calories burned is then used to create your starting suggested calorie intake that is proposed to allow you to reach your goal on the date you desire. Calorie intake or burn numbers are not required to be 100% accurate NOR is there ANY method that can establish this accuracy. Accurate calorie changes, if necessary, to stay on goal are dictated by your weekly fat/weight changes & ongoing trend.
>
> Your Measurement Inputs & Goal Date Determine the Ongoing Accurate Program Feedback
> Your measurement/weight changes from the beginning of your program are mapped to your goal date at each entry creating the program feedback as in the example below:
> “According to your weight/measurement change since the beginning of this program, you lost3lbs therefore you consumed an average of 400calories/day less than you burned during this period. Your goal was to lose 5lbs.To reach your goal your goal by the date you set, you now need to consume 600c/d less than you burn or change your goal date”.
>
> As you can see from the above hypothetical scenario, there is no mention of how many calories you burned or consumed, only what you ABSOLUTELY need to do based on a TRUE number: your measurement/weight change tied to the laws of energy. After 2-3 weeks of participating with the program there is no room for error because the calorie deficit recommendation is always based on your weight trend since the beginning of your current program not the single week to week input (i.e. updated weight is always mapped to your starting weight). Using the “since beginning” weight establishes a true trend thus eliminating any daily fluid fluctuation issues.
>
> Summary
> No matter how you determine your estimated calorie burn and recorded calorie intake, those numbers will always be subject to human error thus so will your estimated calculated deficit. Your dotFIT program ACTUAL deficit is a true number based on a body measurement change used within the indisputable laws of energy – i.e. no room for error. The amount of our ACTUAL deficit is solely responsible for your rate of weight/fat loss – the greater the ACTUAL deficit the faster the weight/fat loss.
>
> Your body measurements/weight change is the answer to your true calorie balance not your inputs or estimates

## G-212 [test] 2026 — (untagged)

**Q:** I'm a 50-year-old man (5'6", 150 lb) estimating 20–25% body fat who has lost 30 lb since last November and is currently eating about 1,400 calories per day but has plateaued; I want to reach 10% body fat — what steps should I add?

*As originally asked:* The goal is 10% body fat to reset metabolism currently at 20-25% estimated. My details 50 yr old male 5’-6” 150lb. 1400 calories daily and have lost 30lb since last November and have stopped loosing weight but have gained substantial muscle. Run a restaurant 4 days a week 10 to 12 hour days Wednesday through Saturday Wake up 6:30 AM, watch the sunrise and get a scoop down of dotfit aminos. Then usually a cup of coffee and try to watch morning mass on YouTube. Then proceeded to take my morning vitamins and supplements, DHEA D3+K. MTHF creatine etc. I am on some peps so I usually inject those as well RETA, MOTS-C Then I usually row on a rowing machine for half an hour about 700 rows very slow no cardio no high heart rate mainly for movement to supplement walking. then jump into my workout around 8:30 or 9 AM X3 band workouts consist of UPPER Mondays and Thursdays consist of laterals ,close grip chest press, over hand back rows, overhead tricep extensions, reverse curls and peck cross overs . 6 exercises Lower, Tuesdays and Saturdays consist of belt squats, stiff leg, dead lift, split squats, ab rotations, and seated calf raises 5 exercises. Three sets each to failure . As I have gotten stumped at 150 pounds and stopped losing fat with a 1400 cal intake. My current coach which has no affiliation to Dotfit tells me to stay with the regimen but i feel it isnt working anymore. Have watched all your recent videos with Niel and his son . Would like to achieve 10% BF . What steps do i need to add ? I have been mostly low carb carnivore but am open to any advice from professionals. Some say lower calories more some say increase calories and time carbs 🤷. Appreciate your time . Juan
