> [NAME], before we get started on your answer:
> Caveat to all numbers is the accuracy of your numbers (e.g., calorie count, body fat, etc.) — but it doesn’t matter; they are just numbers you have assigned to where you are today or placeholders for a starting place. Because you have found your maintenance, that is the spot we work from — the ongoing body fat directional changes and actual overall body composition numbers will capture the true results regardless of your numbers — i.e., we are looking at actual changes to measure progress so we can add, subtract, etc. to move in the right direction until we reach the goal.
>
> Let me break this down for you.
>
> First off — respect. Seriously. Losing 30 pounds since November, building substantial muscle, running a restaurant 4 days a week on 10–12 hour shifts, AND maintaining a structured morning routine with training? That's not a guy who lacks discipline. That's a guy who's hitting a very specific, very solvable problem.
>
> Here's the thing — what you're experiencing is textbook metabolic adaptation, and the good news is we know exactly what's happening and what to do about it.
>
> What's actually going on
> At 150 lb and 5'6", you're not a big guy. You've lost 30 lb, built muscle, and your body has fully adapted to “1,400 calories” (or whatever your weekly average caloric intake is in the past weeks). That IS now your maintenance level. Your metabolism has slightly downregulated to match your intake, including adapting to a lower body weight, so you naturally burn fewer calories doing the same activities you did when you were at a higher body weight. It's not broken — it's actually doing exactly what it's designed to do. It protected you. Now we have to outsmart it.
>
> The "stay the course" advice from your current coach? Look, I'm not here to throw anyone under the bus, but when the data is telling you something isn't working anymore, you don't just stay the course. You adjust. That's the whole game.
>
> Your numbers first
> Let me give you some context here. At 150 lb and roughly 20–25% body fat, you're carrying approximately 112–120 lb of lean body mass (LBM) and 30–37 lb of body fat.
>
> To get to 10% body fat, assuming you maintain or even add a little muscle, you're looking at getting down to roughly 125–130 lb total weight — or building enough muscle that 10% body fat sits at a higher scale weight. Either way, you've got real work ahead, but it's absolutely achievable.
>
> Here's my breakdown — step by step
> 1. Stop thinking about the scale and start measuring body fat every two weeks
> The scale is lying to you right now. You said it yourself — you've gained substantial muscle. That muscle is probably masking some fat loss on the scale. Get a reliable body fat measurement method (DEXA if you can access it, or a good caliper protocol) and track that every two weeks. That's your real progress marker. Weight is almost meaningless at your stage.
>
> 2. You need a calorie recalculation — but probably NOT another cut
> Here's where I'm going to push back on both camps — the "cut more" crowd AND the "just eat more" crowd. At 1,400 calories, you're already very low for a 50-year-old active male running a restaurant. Cutting further risks:
> - Accelerated muscle loss
> - Further metabolic suppression
> - Hormonal disruption (especially important given your peptide protocol and DHEA use)
> - Feeling like garbage, which tanks your performance and adherence
> The bottom line is — you've established “1,400” as your new maintenance. The move isn't necessarily to drop to 1,100–1,200. That's a dangerous road at your size and activity level.
>
> 3. Strategic calorie cycling is your next move
> This is what I'd look at seriously. Instead of a flat 1,400 every day, consider:
> - Training days (Mon, Tue, Thu, Sat): bump to 1,600–1,700 calories, with the added calories coming from targeted carbohydrates around your workout window
> - Non-training/restaurant work days (Wed, Fri, Sun): keep at 1,300–1,400, staying lower carb/carnivore (lean proteins — but get some very low-calorie veggies in)
>
> This does two things — it gives your metabolism a signal that starvation isn't happening (preventing further adaptation), AND it puts fuel exactly where it needs to go: into your training. You're on a low-carb carnivore base, which can be fine, but on training days your muscles want glucose. Even a targeted 50–100 g of quality carbs around your workout can meaningfully change your body composition trajectory and help your peptides work better.
>
> 4. Your protein needs to be non-negotiable
> At your LBM, you need at minimum 112–120 g of protein daily, but ideally closer to 130–150 g. On a carnivore-leaning 1,400 calorie diet, this is achievable but you need to be hitting it consistently. Protein is the most important macro for preserving and building muscle while in a deficit. It also has the highest thermic effect — your body burns more calories just digesting it.
>
> Your dotFIT Aminos in the morning is a great start. Make sure you're counting that toward your total (2 scoops can be counted as ~30 g in your case toward your daily total — but no more regardless of how much you use). Get protein in at every meal or feeding opportunity.
>
> 5. Address the metabolic reset with a short diet break
> This is something the research supports strongly. After an extended period of caloric restriction (and you've been at this since November), your leptin drops, your thyroid slows, your metabolism adapts. A 1–2 week diet break at true maintenance — which for you might actually be 1,800–2,000 calories based on your activity — can partially restore metabolic rate before you re-enter a deficit. This isn't quitting. This is strategy.
>
> 6. Add NEAT — your restaurant days are actually an asset
> Here's something people miss. You're on your feet 10–12 hours in a restaurant 4 days a week. That's significant non-exercise activity thermogenesis (NEAT). Make sure you're not unconsciously compensating by sitting more on your off days. On your off days, prioritize walking. Even 30–45 minutes of easy walking adds meaningful caloric expenditure without touching your recovery or muscle mass. Your rowing machine baseline is great for movement — keep that. But add deliberate walking on off days (use a Fit-Bit/phone or any step counter).
>
> 7. Your training needs a progressive overload check
> X3 bands to failure is solid. But here's my question — are you actually progressing? Meaning, is each session getting harder in some measurable way? More reps, longer time under tension, shorter rest periods, thicker bands? If you've been doing the same exercises at the same resistance for weeks, your body has adapted to that stimulus too. The muscle-building signal has to keep coming. Talk to your coach about how you're tracking progression in those movements.
>
> 8. Build a proper foundation with your supplement stack
> You've got some solid pieces in place — DHEA, D3+K, MTHF, creatine (excellent for body composition and strength at your age), and the peptides. But I want to make sure you've got a complete multivitamin/mineral formula locked in as your foundation. When you're in a caloric deficit, micronutrient gaps are real and they affect everything — energy, recovery, hormonal function, sleep.
>
> I'd strongly recommend the dotFIT All-in-1 SuperBlend as your comprehensive multi base. At 50, in a deficit, training hard, running a restaurant — your micronutrient demands are high and your food variety may not be covering all the gaps, especially on a carnivore-leaning approach. The All-in-1 SuperBlend is designed for active people with serious goals and covers those bases properly alongside everything else you're doing. Take 1 scoop in the AM and one in the PM.
>
> 9. Consider adding the dotFIT Weight Loss & Liver Support stack
> Here's something directly relevant to your situation. At your stage — pushing past a plateau toward a lean body composition goal — adding a liver support and fat metabolism product like dotFIT Weight Loss & Liver Support can be genuinely useful. Your liver is central to fat metabolism, and supporting it matters especially when you're coming off an extended deficit and working long restaurant days. Pair that with LeanMeal as a structured meal replacement to nail one or two of your daily feedings with precision — protein, fiber, controlled calories. It removes the guesswork on those busy restaurant days when you might grab whatever's available. Take 2 shakes daily, added to 2 traditional food meals to help in calorie accuracy and daily energy levels.
>
> 10. Get real data with consistent tracking for the next 30 days
> Before you make any big changes, spend the next 2–3 weeks doing one thing: tracking everything with precision. I mean everything. Use an app, weigh your food if you need to on days off, log honestly. You might think you're at 1,400, but restaurant environments are notorious for untracked bites, tastes, and samples. Once you have clean data, you'll know for certain what you're working with and can make a truly informed adjustment.
>
> The bottom line for [NAME]
> You've done the hard part — you've built a foundation of muscle that most guys your age haven't got. Now we're talking about precision, not punishment. The answer isn't just "eat less" or "blindly." And finally, when we achieve the goal, the calories will go up to a new maintenance and the workload can be lessened so our calories out and in are stable.
>
> Keep us updated.

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. The AminoFormula would be an ideal pre/during and post-workout formula for the goal of bodyfat/weight loss reduction. Therefore you can add it to your baseline dietary support program as shown below.
>
> Thanks again and have a great Sunday.
>
> AminoFormula in Baseline to Control Body Composition
>
> General Health, Recovery, Weight Control and Performance Supplement Program with AminoFormula
> Click on product links for a brief product description including short video
>
> Daily:
> - dotFIT Multivitamin & Mineral for your age and gender. If 50yrs or older use Over50 unless noted below; if female under 50 use Women’s; Males and very active females (competitive training protocols) under 50 use 2-Active, with both genders continuing use till 65yrs if still training competitively at which time every person will use Over50; children under 12yr use Kids; children 12-17yr use 1-Active); Or the Alln1 SuperBlend™
> - Take as directed with meals
> - WheySmooth or other favorite dotFIT protein mix (based on caloric allotment for goal)
> - Use as directed to make sure you get ~1gm of protein per pound of LBM (or weight if not overweight) daily from all sources including foods divided 3-5 times daily
> - Can be used as a supplement to a meal or by itself (or mixed with desired supporting contents) to shore up total daily protein needs
>
> As needed:
> - Super Calcium (read all food labels for calcium content including dotFIT protein shakes)*
> - Use if you do not meet the daily needs of calcium (~1000-1200 mgs) from food/shake intake
> - Females take 1 or 2 daily with meals; if you need to take 2, take one with AM meal and the 2nd with PM meal; Males take 1 only if necessary
> - Super Omega 3
> - Take 1-2 daily with meal if not consuming 8-16oz/wk fatty fish (take 1 if >8oz/wk but <16; 2 if ≤8oz); not necessary if consuming ≥16oz/wk unless for athletic recovery purposes, then increase as directed
>
> *All food labels including Protein Powders must contain calcium levels by serving so always check
>
> Workout Days:
> - AminoFormula (AF) – count as 25-40gms protein
> - Take ½-1 scoop ~10 minutes before and may continue to drink during first 20 minutes of workout (or to midpoint)
> - Take ½-1 scoop immediately following workout
> - Or use both scoops and start ingesting 10 min before workout and consume at a pace so it’s finished at end of activity

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. Your protein needs are simple: Make sure you get ~1gm of protein per pound of LBM (or weight if not overweight) daily from all sources including foods divided 3-5 times daily. If you are using AminoFormula (AF) for exercise recovery, the most you can count it towards your protein goal, no matter how much you use, is 40gms (2-scoops split). See below for proper use within your baseline dietary support program and exercise. Below that is a related Q&A. All that said, you can also use AF to fortify a low protein meal/diet/foods such as plant-based diets, low protein breakfast, etc., to raise the meal’s protein content with caloric efficiency or breaking a fast.
>
> Thanks again and have great day.
>
> AminoFormula in Baseline to Control Body Composition – No Stacking
>
> General Health, Recovery, Weight Control and Performance Supplement Program with AminoFormula
>
> Click on product links for a brief product description including short video
>
> Daily:
>
> dotFIT Multivitamin & Mineral for your age and gender. If 50yrs or older use Over50 unless noted below; if female under 50 use Women’s; Males and very active females (competitive training protocols) under 50 use 2-Active, with both genders continuing use till 65yrs if still training competitively at which time every person will use Over50; children under 12yr use Kids; children 12-17yr use 1-Active); Or the Alln1 SuperBlend™
>
> Take as directed with meals
>
> WheySmooth or other favorite dotFIT protein mix (based on caloric allotment for goal)
>
> Use as directed to make sure you get ~1gm of protein per pound of LBM (or weight if not overweight) daily from all sources including foods divided 3-5 times daily
>
> Can be used as a supplement to a meal or by itself (or mixed with desired supporting contents) to shore up total daily protein needs
>
> As needed:
>
> Super Calcium (read all food labels for calcium content including dotFIT protein shakes)*
>
> Use if you do not meet the daily needs of calcium (~1000-1200 mgs) from food/shake intake
>
> Females take 1 or 2 daily with meals; if you need to take 2, take one with AM meal and the 2nd with PM meal; Males take 1 only if necessary
>
> Super Omega 3
>
> Take 1-2 daily with meal if not consuming 8-16oz/wk fatty fish (take 1 if >8oz/wk but <16; 2 if ≤8oz); not necessary if consuming ≥16oz/wk unless for athletic recovery purposes, then increase as directed
>
> *All food labels including Protein Powders must contain calcium levels by serving so always check
>
> Workout Days:
>
> AminoFormula (AF) –count as 25-40gms protein
>
> Take ½-1scoop ~10 minutes before and may continue to drink during first 20 minutes of workout (or to midpoint)
>
> Take ½-1scoop immediately following workout
>
> Or use both scoops and start ingesting 10min before workout and consume at a pace so it’s finished at end of activity
>
> Q: There is no protein listed on AminoFormula (AF), can I count it as a protein?
>
> A: You may add 25-40gm (1-2scoops) of protein to your daily protein intake when using AF as directed, but generally no more regardless of daily intake. The reason AminoFormula (AF) does not publish a protein amount is because it is purposely not a complete protein as it contains only the essential amino acids that are directly responsible for protein synthesis activation in proportion to the demand, and therefore serves as a low calorie, fast acting, high anabolic signaling product for activating muscle protein synthesis (MPS) beyond intact protein itself.
>
> Your total food/shake protein intake throughout the day supplies the remaining amino acids (and they can be made in the body) necessary for the complete and prolonged synthesis. That said, AF can be used to fortify a low protein meal/food (e.g., plant based, low protein breakfast etc.,) to raise the total food protein biological value (BV) - i.e., eliminate the AA limiting factor for MPS indigenous to weaker protein sources. Further, AF is vegan friendly.
>
> More info:
>
> AF serves to enhance the MPS process beyond protein alone or serve as a low calorie, high anabolic extra source of protein. A protein equivalent in 1-scoop of AF, is generally counted as ~25gms, as it takes ~25-35gms of a complete protein to get the amount of the EAA found in one serving of AF including the 6gms of BCAA. However, regardless of your total AF intake, we advise you only add it as 25-40gm of protein to your 1gm/LB/lean body mass(LBM)/day of protein needs. Mindful that active strength/performance athletes still maintain the 1gm/LB/LBM/D of whole/intact protein sources because different whole food proteins have other helpful properties/nutrients including satiety.

## G-219 [dev] 2026 — Active MV

**Q:** I dieted down to about 11–12% body fat but then had a 2-day binge that increased my weight from 188 to 204.6 lb. How can I keep the physique I built, enjoy some favorite foods on weekends without losing control, and stop feeling constantly hungry after dieting?

*As originally asked:* Hey I recently dieted to my leanest ever of about 11 to 12% body fat realistically. I dieted pretty hard because I had a wedding to go to. I sadly love food and went on a crazy weekend binge where I lost control and went from 188 lbs to 204.6 lbs in just 2 days. I really wanted to keep that lean physique I built but feel like I lost all of my progress. I also want to find a way to enjoy eating some of my favorite foods on the weekend without feeling like I’m going to go crazy and eat everything in sight. Ever since dieting down all I can think of is food and can’t seem to ever stop feeling hungry unless I eat until I’m uncomfortably full.

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. What you are experiencing is what many people go through after dieting to reach a lower body fat. They reach it and celebrate and then find themselves having an ongoing difficult relationship with food. The more aggressive the weight loss (continuous calorie restrictions leading to >2 LB/week), the more common this relationship with foods becomes. The primary reason for successful weight loss is the trigger event — i.e., what motivated you in the first place, such as the wedding in your case. You need to find another motivating factor for the rest of your life and then follow the weekly average calorie intake that keeps you at the goal weight/body fat.
>
> Meaning you can incorporate the foods you like (on weekends or whatever day) within those weekly calories. Example: if your maintenance calories are ~2500 calories/day to maintain your desired body fat after you have reached your goal, you can consume an average of 2200 calories/day during the week, leaving you 5 days × 300 calories = 1500 calories extra for favorite foods on top of the normal calorie intake. And remember, you can always move more (walking, playing sports, biking, etc.) to offset the extra calorie intake, especially on the weekends.
>
> The majority of us former athletes where our sport required regular aggressive dieting (e.g., bodybuilding, wrestling, jockeys, combat sports, etc.) have ongoing problematic relationships with foods (we call it diet PTSD) that we control based on lifelong fitness goals. For me, a retired bodybuilder and now fitness/nutrition leader, I still make a living by being in shape. And you too will be able to control your overall calorie intake if you had to “support your family” by doing so.
>
> So find your motivation, log your foods for at least 90 days, lose no more than 1 LB/week until you reach your goal, at which time your calories will be adjusted to your maintenance. Assuming you are not a member of one of our licensed facilities, you can use the dotFIT program to accomplish all this. It contains the diet information you need to get started and stay on track with your caloric intake/menu planning, etc. It will also give a dietary support program (see below) to help you stick to the plan and reach your goal healthily. I’m also connecting you here with [NAME] (certified counselor and cc’d here) so as necessary, [NAME] can get you pointed in the right direction including proper access to the tools you need. And finally, at the bottom of the email, you will see some tips and tricks you may find helpful.
>
> Hope this all helps!
> Thanks again and have a great day.
>
> ThermAccel in Baseline
> Links will take you to a brief product description including video
>
> Daily:
> - dotFIT Multivitamin & Mineral for your age and gender. If 50 yrs or older use Over50 unless noted below; if female under 50 use Women’s; males and very active females (competitive training protocols) under 50 use 2-Active, with both genders continuing use till 65 yrs if still training competitively at which time every person will use Over50; children under 12 yr use Kids; children 12-17 yr use 1-Active.
> - Take as directed with meals
>
> ThermAccel (TA)
> - Take 2 tablets at breakfast and 2 with lunch, accompanied by at least 8 oz of fluids. Individuals sensitive to caffeine should begin with 1 tab twice daily to assess tolerance before increasing to the maximum dose of 4 tablets daily. Do not use TA with other stimulants or by individuals taking heart medications. Once fat/weight loss goals are achieved and lifestyle changes are firmly in place, ThermAccel can be discontinued.
>
> Daily as needed:
> - Favorite dotFIT protein mix
>   - Use as directed to make sure you get ~1 gm of protein per pound of LBM (or weight if not overweight) daily from all sources including foods divided 3–5 times daily
>   - If exercising, use as directed to get ~25–35 gm protein 30–40 min before workout & repeat dose immediately post workout (see below)
>   - Can be used as a supplement to a meal or by itself (or mixed with desired supporting contents) to shore up total daily protein needs
> - SuperCalcium (read all food and shake labels)
>   - Use if you do not meet the daily needs of calcium (~1000–1200 mg) from food/shake intake
>   - Females take 1 or 2 daily with meals; if you need to take 2, take one with AM meal and the 2nd with PM meal; males take 1 only if necessary
> - SuperOmega-3
>   - Take 1–2 daily with meal if not consuming 8–16 oz/week fatty fish (take 1 if >8 oz/week but <16; 2 if ≤8 oz); not necessary if consuming ≥16 oz/week unless for athletic recovery purposes
>
> Workout days
> - dotFIT Protein of choice*. Use as directed to get:
>   - 25–35 gm protein 30–40 min before workout & repeat same dose immediately following exercise
>   - *May substitute favorite dotFIT Nutrition High Protein Bar based on venue convenience
>
> Here are your natural options that have been highly successful in curbing late night appetites including the proverbial sweet tooth:
> - Combine Weight Loss and Liver Support (WLLS) with ThermAccel and take daily as directed
> - Use the Alln1 SuperBlend™ instead of your current multivitamin and mineral formula (and any other supplements it replaces) and take 1 scoop in AM and 1 in PM to cut the late night appetite
> - The most popular way of incorporating Alln1 SuperBlend™ into lifestyle is 1 scoop combined with 1 serving of your favorite vanilla protein, fruit, ice and water. Take other dose as desired (often taken with water/favorite fluid and ice in evening to help control appetite (may add ½–1 scoop AminoFormula if needed)).
>
> Incorporate bodybuilder tricks at night:
> - Brush your teeth after your last meal (sends a signal to the brain you’re done).
> - Chew sugarless gum as your dessert.
> - Drink a diet soda for dessert.
> - Mix AminoFormula with a low calorie beverage and drink it for your dessert.
> - Save your last calories for a low calorie dessert such as low calorie frozen yogurt or a protein bar.

>   - Females take 1 or 2 daily with meals; if you need to take 2, take one with AM meal and the 2nd with PM meal; Males take 1 only if necessary
> - Omega 3
>   - Take 1-2 daily with meal if not consuming 8-16oz/wk fatty fish (take 1 if >8oz/wk but <16; 2 if ≤8oz); not necessary if consuming ≥16oz/wk unless for athletic recovery purposes, then increase as directed

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. First, your desired deficit of 1000c/d is not a good idea if you want to preserve/build muscle while losing bodyfat. Manage the deficit to average ~500 calories/d (adjust your calories in and/or out as needed to maintain this average (see below for monitoring and adjusting for continuous bodyfat reduction). And make sure you monitor your bodyfat with your weight because with the programs we deliver, people put on muscle while losing bodyfat, so body weight alone does not tell the true body composition change story (plus we added CreatineMonohydrate to your baseline supplement program below since you are an experienced exerciser). The dotFIT program contains the diet and self-monitoring information you need to get started and stay on track with your caloric intake/menu planning, etc., as you pursue your body composition/fitness goals.
>
> Assuming you are not a member of one of our licensed facilities, I’m connecting you here with [NAME] (certified counselor and cc’d here) so he can get you pointed in the right direction including proper access to the tools you need. Below is the baseline dietary support program (added CreatineMonohydrate) for all active people that want to remain active that forms the foundation for health, daily recovery and exercise-induced gains so that you can protect and continue to build lean body mass (LBM). After using the baseline supplement program, and you want to take your fitness goals further, you can use our supplement screening tool to get the proper recommendations or simply contact [NAME].
>
> Thanks again and have a great day. We will be with you all the way on your fitness journey.
>
> Level – 1 Creatine in Baseline for Optimizing Body Composition with Health & Performance
> Click on product links for a brief product description including short video
>
> Daily:
>
> dotFIT Multivitamin & Mineral for your age and gender: use 2-Active, OR Alln1 SuperBlendTM
> Take as directed with meals
>
> Daily as needed:
>
> WheySmooth
> Use as directed to make sure you get ~1gm of protein per pound of LBM (or weight if not overweight) from all sources including foods divided 3-5 times daily
> Can be used as a supplement to a meal or by itself (or mixed with desired supporting contents) to shore up total daily protein & calorie needs
>
> Omega 3
> Take 1-2 daily with meal if not consuming 8-16oz/wk fatty fish (take 1 if >8oz/wk but <16; 2 if ≤8oz); not necessary if consuming ≥16oz/wk unless for athletic recovery purposes, then increase as directed
>
> Calcium Complex (read all food labels for calcium content)
> Use if you do not meet the daily needs of calcium (~1000-1200 mgs) from food/shake intake
> Females take 1 or 2 daily with meals; if you need to take 2, take one with AM meal and the 2nd with PM meal; Males take 1 only if necessary
>
> 2nd Week Start CreatineMonohydrate (CrM)*
>
> CreatineMonohydrate Loading (true loading =.14g/lb/d) & Maintenance (true maintenance .045g/lb[LBM]/d)* protocol
> Mix 1 scoop (5gms) with 4-8oz of favorite fluid/shake and take 4 times daily with a protein and/or carbohydrate containing meal/drink for first 5 days. Thereafter take 1-scoop twice daily to maintain stores. To help maximize creatine uptake, split doses throughout the day with meals/drinks containing protein and/or carbohydrate
> Training days: 1 dose before workout & 1 after with meals/drinks. May mix with your pre & post training formula
> Non-training days take 1-dose with AM meal and 1-dose with PM meal
>
> Workout days
>
> WheySmooth
> Take as directed (25-35gm protein) 30-40min before workout & repeat immediately post exercise
>
> Creatine Monohydrate
> On training days, use one dose before workout and one after with meals/drinks. May mix with your pre & post training formula
>
> *When not dosing by body weight and under 175LBS, may cut daily maintenance dose in half - i.e., after loading period, take a total of 5gms daily and split as described
>
> Monitoring and Adjustments:
>
> Measurable or visual reduction in body fat and/or weight proceeds in a consistent manner, e.g. a decrease in circumference inches, and/or the desired average decrease in body fat per week
> If progress stops or slows dramatically, use one or combination of adjustments to re-start the process:
>
> Increase daily activities (e.g., daily steps or other non-athletic/exercise activities)
> Standing and pacing burns 1.5-2 times more calories than sitting for the same period
> There are approximately 2000-2500 steps (depending on stride length) in a mile. Walking 2000 steps will burn ~75-150 more calories (depending on individual size) than sitting for the same time, takes ~20-30min and can be done anywhere, even in the office, while on the phone or watching TV
>
> Increase cardio workout time and/or intensity and include interval training if necessary
> Ex: if doing 30 minutes 5D/WK, go to 45min and add interval work within the time frame
>
> Decrease food intake approximately 200 to 300 calories per day or remove a small portion (carbs/fats) of largest meal other than pre-workout meal (incorporate 3-days low/1-high as necessary)
>
> Maximize dietary support beyond baseline as necessary (muscle building, thermogenic/energy partitioning, etc.)
>
> Repeat the process any time body fat is stable for at least one week. Always remember if you are supplementing properly and you stop losing fat, you need to eat less, move/burn more or a combination of the two regardless of what you read or hear from others. As you lower calorie intake, make sure your protein intake stays at or above 1gm per pound of body weight to protect LBM

> FAQ
> Q: What is the difference between the dotFIT ExtremeCreatineXXXL and CreatineMonohydrate?
> A: CreatineMonohydrate is simply the creatine monohydrate. ExtremeCreatineXXXL is the same creatine monohydrate (NSF-CS) but also contains beta-alanine, glutamine and Carflow™ (vasodilator) for their respective additive performance benefits. For a product description including short video go to the dotFIT website store and click on the product you would like to learn about. > Dosing ExtremeCreatineXXXL+ as a Standalone Product
> ExtremeCreatineXXXL+ to maintain all till end of cycle
> On training days, take one (1) scoop before training and one (1) scoop after. You may use along with your pre- and post-workout shake or a meal containing protein and/or carbohydrate.
> For those users >150LBS and seeking to maximize performance such as strength, speed and power activities, add a third scoop any other time of the day with a meal or shake.
> On non-training days (to maintain Cr, BA & CF) levels, take one (1) scoop with morning meal or shake and one (1) scoop with evening meal or shake. If using three (3) scoops, spread each scoop throughout the day with meals/shake – i.e., morning, afternoon and early evening. (true Creatine only supplementation maintenance is .045g/lb/d).
>
> Dosing ExtremeCreatineXXXL+ within Size and Performance Stacking Protocols See complete supplement stacking programs for competitive size and performance athletes here: Creatine Muscle & Performance Stacks
>
> CreatineMonohydrate as a standalone product

> CreatineMonohydrate Loading (true loading .14g/lb/d) & Maintenance* Strategy (true maintenance .045g/lb/d)
> Mix 1 scoop (5gms) with 4-8oz of favorite fluid/shake and take 4 times daily with a protein and/or carbohydrate containing meal/drink for first 5 days. Thereafter take 1-scoop twice daily to maintain stores. To help maximize creatine uptake, split doses throughout the day with meals/drinks containing protein and/or carbohydrate.

> Non-training days take 1-scoop with AM meal and 1-scoop with PM meal.
> *If under 175LBS you can cut the daily maintenance dose in half - i.e., after loading period, take a total of 5gms daily and split as described or all in 1 dose daily.
>
> Q: I wanted to double check and see if NO7 Rage3 and Extreme Creatine formulas can both be used together since they both have creatine in them?
> A: You can use the NO7 PREWORKOUT (formerly Rage) along with ExtremeCreatineXXXL (EC) and this is common. *The goal is when using NO7 and EC together, you need to maintain 5gms creatine daily between the 2 products if under 175LBS, and 7.5-10gms of creatine daily if ≥over 175LBS and it doesn’t hurt to over, but it is more economical for you to add the daily amounts when using both. See below for best use when using both products in the baseline dietary support program.
>
> NO7 PREWORKOUT (NO) & ExtremeCreatineXXXL (EC) Performance & Size Supplement Plan in Baseline
> ActiveMV - Multivitamin & Mineral Formula
>  Take two (2) daily: one (1) immediately following first large meal and one (1) after final meal of the day. Or use Alln1 SuperBlend™.

>  Take 1-2 daily with meal if not consuming 8-16oz/wk fatty fish (take 1 if >8oz/wk but <16; 2 if ≤8oz); not necessary if consuming ≥16oz/wk unless for athletic recovery purposes, then increase as directed.
> FirstString (or WheySmooth depending on daily calorie allotment - take as directed)
>  Take two (2) scoops 30-45 minutes before workout or use favorite dotFIT® bar for convenience.
>  Take two (2) scoops after workout.
>  Use anytime throughout the day as a supplement to a meal or by itself mixed with desired ingredients to add protein/calories as needed to meet daily requirements.
>  Be sure to ingest approximately one (1) g of protein per pound of lean body mass (or weight if not overweight) daily from all sources including foods divided 4-5 times daily along with the pre/post shakes (the pre/post shakes combined generally supply 40-50 g of the daily requirement).
>
> ExtremeCreatineXXXL+ to maintain all till end of cycle*
>  On training days, take 1-scoop after training (NO7 has necessary pre-training ingredients). You may use along with your post workout shake or meal containing protein and/or carbohydrate. For users >150LBS, seeking to maximize performance such as strength, speed and power activities, add a second scoop any other time of the day with a meal or shake.
>  On non-training days (to maintain Cr, BA & Careflow) levels, take 1-scoop with AM meal or shake & 1-scoop with PM meal/shake. If using three (3) scoops, spread each scoop throughout the day with meals/shake – i.e., morning, afternoon and early evening. (true Creatine only maintenance is .045g/lb/d).
> NO7 PREWORKOUT (workout days only b/c EC daily maintains Cr & BA saturation)
>  Take 1.5-2.5 scoops depending on weight (if caffeine sensitive, start lower dose and increase to proper weight dose if not uncomfortably affected by caffeine) approximately 10 minutes before workout. See directions on label for weight dosages.
> NO7 PREWORKOUT contains 150 mg of caffeine per scoop. As a reference: Starbucks Grande-drip coffee contains ~330 mg of caffeine, close to the same amount 2scoops of NO7Rage.
>

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Always great to see you — albeit on camera. 😊 The short answer is that the vast majority of dotFIT clients would rarely need a separate magnesium supplement if they are following their proper baseline supplementation and adding other dotFIT products as needed. And yes, the Tolerable Upper Limit Levels (UL) of magnesium is only 350 mg. But as you mentioned, that only means your supplemental magnesium should supposedly not exceed that number. However, spreading it out like you are, it is fine to go slightly over that UL (see below for more info from our Sleep Aid formula lectures). I also place a related Q & A below that might be helpful.
>
> Hope this helps — and have a great evening. Reach out anytime!
>
> [NAME] et al. 2024-Current Evidence on Common Dietary Supplements for Sleep Quality
> With regards to sleep, Magnesium (MG) has several essential functions that include: GABA activation (calming neurotransmitter), increases in melatonin production, muscle relaxation, regulation of stress-responses, regulation of calcium levels which promote muscle contraction and relaxation, and anti-anxiety effects. Furthermore, low magnesium status has been associated with chronic inflammatory stress in both animal and human research studies. However, despite its popularity, there are few clinical benefits to MG supplementation (MGS) in individuals with adequate serum magnesium. There is a potential for MGS in individuals with low serum magnesium to improve sleep quality.
> Our world (dotFIT) should not be MG deficient/insufficient, therefore the 100mg add from Sleep Aid is safe and appropriate*
>
> 2 Active: 200mg
> 1 Woman’s: 100mg (b/c most need Ca supp)
> 2 Over50: 150mg
> 1 svg SuperBlend: 160mg
> 1 SuperCalcium: 125mg
> 2 Sleep Aid: 100mg
>
> *UL of 350mg from supps is generally disregarded because it’s based on a single dose, not spread out as ours would be. Further, the Ca/mg ratio should be protected (2-3:1)
>
> Final Conclusion/takeaway: Meet ~400-500mg/d through diet and supplement intake that includes a complete MVM containing MG and the other ~20 known under-consumed VMs: dotFIT MVM/Alln1 SuperBlend™ = 100-200mg; Sleep Aid =100mg; diet = 250mg; Daily total is ~450-550mg MG (2-300mg in supps), which would maximize MGs basic role in sleep quality mechanisms, making high dose separate MGS unnecessary and likely ineffective.
>
> MG unique contribution: achieving proper MG intake as described here, offers a unique contribution to the enhancement of all the Sleep Aid’s ingredients mechanisms of action related to stress relief, mood, sleep quality and overall health since MG an essential mineral active in these myriad pathways and when shorted, all systems suffer.
>
> Q: Will you all sell and magnesium supplement? Also, if my doctor is prescribing that I take one, are there any legit options? Not sure if you can answer that for me, but thank you for your time
>
> A: Thanks for contacting us. We will not be making a separate magnesium supplement any time soon, and only because we do not have a large enough market in our distribution channel (sport and fitness and Practitioner channels) to support the large product runs necessary to be cost effective. And also because we have magnesium in all our multivitamin and mineral formula (MVM), Alln1 SuperBlend™ , SuperCalcium and some other products. Meaning for the majority of our users, when combining diet and supplements, would not need more magnesium, or they would be over the Tolerable Upper Limit Levels (UL). Of course there may be exceptions, such as yourself where a doctor has prescribed additional magnesium for something they diagnosed.
>
> When searching for a separate magnesium supplement, look to the NSF website or another third-party certification.
>
> [NAME]

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. The Alln1 SuperBlend™ (SB) contains all you need for a multivitamin and mineral formula (MVM) and contains a plant omega-3, which is different than the marine omega-3 you are currently using. The SB, of course, contains many other health-promoting nutrients as it includes a full day's recommendation for: vitamins, minerals, antioxidants, probiotics, prebiotics, ashwagandha, turmeric & mushroom blend, along with 6 g of fiber and 2 full servings of vegetables.
>
> Calcium and creatine are not in the SB. Below are related FAQs that cover proper usage with dotFIT supplements.
>
> Thanks again and have a great day.
>
> FAQs and more information
>
> Q: If I order the Alln1 Super Blend, does that replace the MV over 50, Super Omega and Super Calcium?
> A: You do not need to take your Over50 multivitamin and mineral formula (MVM) when using the Alln1 SuperBlend™ (SB). Although the SB contains omega-3s, it is a plant source, and thus acts slightly differently in the body than a marine source, such as the SuperOmega-3. Therefore, you can continue using your SuperOmega if you do not consume fatty fish regularly and your SuperCalcium if you are not getting 1200 mg/day from diet.
>
> Q: If someone is using the dotFIT health supplements and wants to switch to the Alln1 SuperBlend™ (SB) what will they no longer need?
> A: They will no longer need the Multivitamin, SuperiorAntioxidant, UltraProbiotic, Digestive Enzymes because those formulas are in the SB. They may add these additional dF health products as needed: SuperOmega (SB has a plant Omega-3 - see below FAQ) and extra V-D (SB contains 1000 IUs but sometimes more is needed to reach >30 ng/ml). If recommended, you can add BrainHealth, JointSkinCollagen+, and SuperCalcium, as these are purposely not in the SB because not everyone of all ages would necessarily need these. All other dotFIT products (e.g., performance, bodyfat/weight loss, proteins, bars, etc.) can be added as usual.
>
> Q: I am taking the Alln1 SuperBlend™ (SB) daily, is there any need for me to keep taking the SuperOmega-3 fish oil?
> A: If you are not eating at least 2 servings/week of fatty fish, then yes, you should continue to take the dotFIT Super Omega-3 because it is a marine Omega-3, which has complementary actions to vegan/plant omega-3 sources. Although the SB contains omega-3s, it is from flaxseed to keep the product vegan friendly for pure vegan clients, giving others the option of adding a marine source. Again, for basic health protection, if you are eating ≥8 oz of fish weekly, you do not need to add the SuperOmega-3 as you will have a healthy dose of all omega-3 sources. For extended athletic purposes (e.g., recovery, performance, body composition, etc.), the dosage of a marine source (Super Omega-3) is generally 3–6 g/day, which would likely require additional supplementation based on fish intake (Example: if eating less than 8 oz/week and using SB, add 3–4 SuperOmega-3s).

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. The Active multivitamin and mineral formula (MVM) is in the Alln1 SuperBlend (SB) along with many other formulas, so no, you do not need to take another MVM. See below for a related FAQ.
>
> Thanks again and have a great day.
>
> Q: If someone is using the dotFIT health supplements and wants to switch to the Alln1 SuperBlend (SB) what will they no longer need?
> A: They will no longer need the Multivitamin, SuperiorAntioxidant, UltraProbiotic, Digestive Enzymes because those formulas are in the SB. They may add these additional dF health products as needed: SuperOmega (SB has a plant Omega-3 - see below FAQ) and extra V-D (SB contains 1000 IUs but sometimes more is needed to reach >30 ng/ml). If recommended, you can add BrainHealth, JointSkinCollagen+, and SuperCalcium, as these are purposely not in the SB because not everyone of all ages would necessarily need these. All other dotFIT products (e.g., performance, bodyfat/weight loss, proteins, bars, etc.) can be added as usual.
>
> Q: I am taking the Alln1 SuperBlend (SB) daily, is there any need for me to keep taking the SuperOmega-3 fish oil?
> A: If you are not eating at least 2svgs/week of fatty fish, then yes, you should continue to take the dotFIT Super Omga-3 because it is marine Omega-3, which has complementary actions to vegan/plant omega-3 sources. Although the SB contains omega-3s, it is from flaxseed to keep the product vegan friendly for pure vegan clients, giving others the option of adding a marine source. Again, for basic health protection, if you are eating ≥8oz of fish weekly, you do not need to add the SuperOmega-3 as you will have a healthy dose of all omega-3 sources. For extended athletic purposes (e.g., recovery, performance, body composition, etc.), the dosage of a marine source (Super Omega-3) is generally 3-6 gm/d, which would likely require additional supplementation based on fish intake (EX: if eating less than 8oz/wk and using SB, add 3-4 SuperOmega-3s).

> Use as directed to make sure you get ~1 gm of protein per pound of LBM (or weight if not overweight) daily from all sources including foods divided 3–5 times daily.

>

> Take 1/2 scoop immediately following the workout.
> Or use both scoops and start ingesting 10 minutes before workout and consume at a pace so it’s finished at the end of activity.
>
> * Most popular way of consuming SB daily is mixing it with water and 25 g of vanilla WheySmooth or if vegan use the BestPlantProtein.
>
> Full day's recommendation for: vitamins and minerals, antioxidants, probiotics, prebiotics, Ashwagandha, turmeric & mushroom blend, along with 6 g of fiber and 2 full servings of vegetables. As desired, add any fruit for the perfect one-and-done health habit.

**Source answer:**

> Hey [CUSTOMER],
> Below is from our Practitioner lectures – also an FAQ and answer that should answer your question.
> Have a great evening.
>
> Hi [NAME],
> Yes, the gentleman is correct; he can use the AminoFormula (AF) in the ways he describes. But as I mentioned in the lecture, only count AminoFormula as 25-40 g (1–2 scoops) of protein toward your daily goal of 1 g per pound of lean body mass (LBM) of protein no matter how much you use. This is because you need traditional food proteins for other health contributions, including satiety. See below FAQ from the lecture and how to use AF as a protein contribution to the diet.
>
> You do not need to pair AminoFormula with NO7 – that is optional.
>
> Thanks and have a great day.
>
> Q: There is no protein listed on AminoFormula (AF), can I count it as a protein?
> A: You may add 25-40 g (1–2 scoops) of protein to your daily protein intake when using AF as directed, but generally no more regardless of daily intake. The reason AminoFormula (AF) does not publish a protein amount is because it is purposely not a complete protein as it contains only the essential amino acids that are directly responsible for protein synthesis activation in proportion to the demand, and therefore serves as a low-calorie, fast-acting, high-anabolic signaling product for activating muscle protein synthesis (MPS) beyond intact protein itself.
>
> Your total food/shake protein intake throughout the day supplies the remaining amino acids (and they can be made in the body) necessary for the complete and prolonged synthesis. That said, AF can be used to fortify a low-protein meal/food (e.g., plant-based, low-protein breakfast, etc.) to raise the total food protein biological value (BV) — i.e., eliminate the amino acid limiting factor for MPS indigenous to weaker protein sources. Further, AF is vegan friendly.
>
> More info:
> AF serves to enhance the MPS process beyond protein alone or serve as a low-calorie, high-anabolic extra source of protein. A protein equivalent in 1 scoop of AF is generally counted as ~25 g, as it takes ~25–35 g of a complete protein to provide the amount of essential amino acids found in one serving of AF, including the 6 g of BCAA. However, regardless of your total AF intake, we advise you only add it as 25–40 g of protein to your 1 g per lb LBM per day protein needs. Be mindful that active strength/performance athletes still maintain the 1 g per lb LBM per day of whole/intact protein sources because different whole food proteins have other helpful properties/nutrients, including satiety.

> Both these answers are okay but here is the bottom line. If she has “loose skin” at her age, nothing much is going to happen to pull it tight (the hormone elastin wanes with age - it is the natural hormone that keeps skin tight in youth). All she can do is add the muscle following the muscle gain plan to increase the size under the skin, and add the Collagen Complex to her baseline supps. Note: the SuppBeast response talks about a small deficit as if her goal was to lose fat and gain muscle, which may be what is helpful in the desired skin look if she has too much body fat — but her main goal should be to add muscle as she requested and body fat should drift down on its own. The true hydration rules are below:
> Fluid Recommendations*
> 16 oz 2Hr before activity (extra 8-16 oz 1hr before on hot days)
> 4-8 oz every 20 minutes during activity
> 20 oz for every pound of weight loss post-activity
> *If necessary (extended training sessions) use electrolyte formula and water as directed; may mix with AminoFormula

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. The Sleep Aid contains no ingredients you mention (shellfish or peanuts/tree nuts). The label has warnings (see below) as necessary if you are worried about a rare cross-contamination issue. I also posted a related Q&A below that has more information.
>
> Thanks again and have a great night.
>
> Sleep Aid label.
>
> Q: Just looking for a clarification on the Digestive Enzymes. It states it's gluten free, but when I received it the bottle states it's manufactured in a place that may make other products with wheat. So, is this product gluten free safe for celiac? Usually, I do not take anything labeled this way due to concerns of cross contamination unless I reach out to the manufacturer. Please advise. Thank you.
>
> A: The simple answer is to read all labels. While none of our products, except for a few nutrition bars, contain gluten or related ingredients, you only need to read all labels carefully if you believe that any possible/unlikely cross contamination from facilities that may make products with gluten/wheat may affect your celiac. All our labels have the correct legal warnings when necessary. See below. If you do not see this warning related to your sensitivity, you are good to go. And while the Digestive Enzymes have that warning, and of course it contains no gluten ingredients, by law you must list if the product is made in a facility that may also use wheat for other products – i.e., in case of some unlikely cross-contamination you may fear based on your sensitivity.
>
> “Allergen Warning: This product was produced in a facility that may also process ingredients containing milk, eggs, fish, shellfish, tree nuts, peanuts, wheat, and soybeans.”

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> See below for the quick answers on all in red. The Creatine guys have obviously not been on my creatine lectures where all is explained about loading or not and maintenance levels. And no clue where they got their .07 gm/lb versus .04 gm/lb numbers or what they mean ([.045 is what we use for maintenance based on real athlete usage]). We have never said loading is necessary – it is simply preferred by competitive athletes to achieve an earlier ergogenic effect.
>
> Loading is .14 gm/LB/BW (I use LBM) and maintenance in conservative science is ~3–5 g/day or 0.03 g/kg/day (.014 gm/LB/d), which is too low for powerful active athletes. I only use LBM for loading and maintenance (lower than body weight but more accurate). Our maintenance doses: 5 g/d or 0.045 g/LB/LBM/d (larger athletes may need 5–10 gm/d* Ref2;#7,8,10,46-48). My recommendations are based on my vast unparalleled experience working with athletes from 450 LBM strongmen all the way to Olympic gymnasts using creatine. And unarguably, no one has more experience than I do with creatine and athletes of all walks.
>
> Below is from our lectures – and by the way, they (your “creatine experts”) are free to set up a quick call with me any time after they have attended the full lecture. The slides below are from the lecture. I also attached the PDF from one (albeit not the newest). There is also a full creatine episode on SuppBeast.
>
> Call me with any more questions.
>
> Keep up the great work!
>
> Practical Interpretation for ≥200 lb Athletes
>
> Mechanistic and review data support the rationale that larger athletes may need 5–10 g/day to maintain saturation 10.
>
> Large metaanalyses show creatine works across a wide range of maintenance doses, with some performance signals in >5–8 g/day ranges 1620.
>
> No substantive safety concerns emerge at these doses in healthy athletes 351019.
>
> Summary Table
>
> | Question | Takeaway for ≥200 lb athletes | Citations |
> | Is 3–5 g/day “enough” for most? | Yes, for typical-sized users | 3519 |
> | Any rationale for 5–10 g/day in big athletes? | Yes, to sustain saturation in larger bodies | 10 |
> | Any performance advantage of >5–8 g/day? | Some evidence (e.g., jump, leg press) | 620 |
> | Safety at 5–10 g/day long term? | Supported in healthy populations | 351019 |
>
> Figure 2 Summary of evidence on higher creatine maintenance doses.
>
> Bottom line: For a 200+ lb strength/power athlete, 5 g/day is clearly supported, and 5–10 g/day is physiologically reasonable and evidence-compatible when aiming to ensure saturation. Further, empirical data supports higher doses in larger athletes than the 1-size-fits-all or low maintenance dose of conservative science.
